# Decision Log
<!-- doc-status: active -->

Record durable methodological and architectural decisions here. Keep entries short enough to scan.

## Template

### YYYY-MM-DD: Decision title

- **Context:** What changed or what result forced a choice?
- **Decision:** What did we decide?
- **Reason:** Why is this the right tradeoff now?

## 2026-09-18: Accept resolution_concordance_1626_1630 as final; stop pursuing missing_htr completeness

- **Context:** `resolution_concordance_1626_1630` (Steps 1-5 in PLAN.md)
  reached 70.7% `resolved_auto`, 28.6% `missing_htr`, 0.6% `nihil_actum`.
  Step 1's diagnostic showed 49.7% of the whole ledger has no HTR session for
  its inventory anywhere within +/-14 days -- a structural coverage ceiling,
  not a matching-quality gap. The Step 5 window-widen follow-up already
  closed the addressable part of that gap (0/475 entity-overlap signal
  before the fix, 125/475 after); the remainder showed no further signal at
  any tested window.
- **Decision:** Accept the current concordance output as final. Do not
  pursue further completeness work on the remaining `missing_htr` rows.
  Attempted a bounded, one-time pagexml spot-check first (inventory 4861,
  100% `missing_htr`; inventory 4562's `1629-05-12` "Crèvecoeur" row) but
  found it is not actually cheap: `docs/DATA.md`'s claim that pagexml lives
  at `/Volumes/2tb disk/data/` is stale -- that directory only holds
  `ead_metadata-extended.tsv` and `emigratie`. The real archive is
  `/Volumes/2tb disk/datasets/republic/source/1.01.02-pagexml.tgz`
  (88.5 GB, unextracted, not registered in `data_manifest.toml`); the
  companion `sessions_json-2026-02-27.tar.gz` (10 GB, also unextracted) sits
  alongside it. Listing/extracting specific inventories from an 88.5 GB
  tarball is real heavy compute, not a quick `ls`/grep, so the spot-check
  itself is deferred rather than run as part of closing this track.
- **Reason:** The wide-window test found essentially zero entity-overlap
  signal at +/-2..+/-7 days on the unresolved rows (475/475 at 0.0 before
  the fix), strong evidence the ceiling reflects absent source coverage
  rather than a fixable matching/extraction defect -- and the archive's
  actual size/state (unextracted, unregistered, un-indexed) makes verifying
  that against pagexml directly a nontrivial project of its own, not a
  sanity check. Chasing full completeness here has demonstrated near-zero
  marginal return; effort is better spent on the separate, still-open HTR
  under-segmentation problem (S4 Segmentation Transfer, 81.1% of
  session-days affected) or the NER training track. If pagexml verification
  is wanted later, it needs its own scoped step: register the archive (or a
  targeted extraction of it) in `data_manifest.toml` first, per project
  convention, rather than reaching into `/Volumes/2tb disk/...` directly.
  `docs/DATA.md`'s pagexml location is also now known-stale and should be
  corrected separately.

## 2026-09-18: Do not start line-level segmentation yet; verify ground-truth coverage first

- **Context:** docs/S4_ITERATION_REVIEW.md flagged line-level cut points (per SEGMENTATION_TRANSFER.md section 9) as the anticipated next unit for the 12 gold days where the paragraph axis is coarser than K_e, citing 'supervision already available upstream' (NeuralLineClassifier, htr_classified_lines.csv, res_start).
- **Decision:** Do not build the line-level DP/classifier pipeline this session. First map the 1,294 checked, target-inventory rows in the marijn-variant CSV to calendar dates and check overlap with the 12 gold days -- a cheap join, not the full pipeline.
- **Reason:** Verified directly: the canonical htr_classified_lines.csv (14,324 rows) has zero rows in target inventories 3185-3189 -- period-skewed to inventory 3156 and ~176 others, same zero-overlap failure res_start already had. The marijn-variant file does cover the right inventories (1,294 checked rows, 121 para_start / 123 para_end) but at spot-check density (2-9 sampled pages per inventory out of hundreds), unmapped to dates, and unlikely to include these specific 12 days. Building the full DP/classifier pipeline (plus resolving the score_typed_overlap vs align_session scoring-convention mismatch, plus new (enriched_id, line_id) entity lookups) on an unverified data assumption risks exactly the disproportionate-effort-for-uncertain-gain outcome flagged when this was scoped -- the res_start precedent shows this corpus's upstream ground truth does not reliably cover the 1626-1630/3185-3189 window.

## 2026-09-18: Diagnose S4 boundary-F1 stagnation as a paragraph-axis granularity ceiling, not a scorer defect

- **Context:** PLAN.md's 2026-09-18 S4 session flagged an open question: boundary micro F1 stayed exactly 0.576 (tol0) / 0.727 (tol2) even though the per-boundary snap-collision fix moved 20 more boundaries from raw interpolation to phrase-snapped positions (6/32 -> 26/32) on the 5 multi-boundary predicted gold days. A per-boundary gold-vs-predicted comparison (reusing existing s4_paragraph_axis_predictions.jsonl and boundary_gold_paragraph_axis, no rerun) found the cause: gold's own boundary annotations repeat the identical paragraph_stream_index for multiple distinct K_e-1 cut points whenever the paragraph axis is coarser than the resolution count (e.g. 1626-02-28: 6 boundary slots collapse onto 1 distinct axis position, axis_count=8 for k_e=7). Across all 17 eligible gold days with any boundary, 16 have at least one such collision; 41/179 gold boundary slots (22.9%) corpus-wide share a position with another slot in the same day.
- **Decision:** Treat boundary_f1_tolerance0/tolerance2 as having hit a structural ceiling from paragraph-level axis granularity, not a defect in interpolation/snapping. Stop trying to move these two metrics via better interpolation or phrase-snapping; that effort is spent. Any further F1 gain requires finer-than-paragraph segmentation, which is the already-scoped line-level-segmentation track (state.json status: blocked, pending htr_classified_lines.csv date-coverage verification). S4's other metrics (predicted-day coverage, corpus baseline coverage, paragraph-axis entity coverage) are unaffected and remain open.
- **Reason:** compute_boundary_prf awards at most one hyp match per distinct ref value, and predicted boundary sequences are always strictly increasing (never repeat a position), so duplicate-valued gold slots impose a hard recall ceiling regardless of predictor quality: max reachable TP = count of distinct ref values, not len(ref). Verified on the 5 multi-boundary predicted days: tol0 TP (19) already reaches 79% of that ceiling (24 distinct values across 34 ref slots) and tol2 TP (24) reaches exactly 100% of it -- the snap-collision fix improved individual boundary position accuracy, but the metric was already saturated against the axis-imposed ceiling both before and after, which is why F1 did not move.

## 2026-09-18: Correct line-level-segmentation blocker: page-to-date join needs new tooling, not a cheap check

- **Context:** docs/SEGMENTATION_TRANSFER.md section 9 and the 2026-09-18 blocked decision described the next step as 'a cheap join' -- map the marijn-variant htr_classified_lines_marijn.csv page_ids (e.g. NL-HaNA_1.01.02_3156_0313) to calendar dates and check overlap with the 12 gold days. Checked what that join would actually require.
- **Decision:** The 'cheap join' framing is wrong and is corrected here. No dataset in data_manifest.toml maps (inventory, scan/page) to date for inventories 3185-3189. session_index_all.parquet and session_date_status_1626_1630 both key on (inventory_id, session_num/enriched_date) only -- no scan/page field. Getting a page_id like ...-page-624 to a calendar date requires opening individual session JSON records inside the unextracted sessions_json-2026-02-27.tar.gz (10 GB, same archive flagged as heavy/deferred in the 2026-09-18 resolution-concordance decision) to read each session's page range, then joining that to session_date_status_1626_1630. This is new tooling over an unextracted 10 GB archive, not a quick lookup. Task stays blocked; do not attempt the join as a quick check in a future session -- scope it as its own step (register a targeted extraction in data_manifest.toml first) or drop it.
- **Reason:** Per ITERATION_POLICY.md's anti-pattern list, a design doc's 'this check is cheap' claim needs verifying against the real files before scoping work around it -- same lesson as the res_start and htr_classified_lines.csv zero-overlap findings already in this doc. Verified directly this session: session_index_all and the session-date ledger are both session-level, not scan/page-level, so the join needs a new page-range extraction step, which is exactly the kind of heavy/unbounded archive work this project's workflow defers to an explicit, scoped step rather than running inline.

## 2026-09-18: Explain the boundary_gold_paragraph_axis data gap for 1626-05-17 and 1627-04-11

- **Context:** docs/S4_ITERATION_REVIEW.md flagged 2 of the 21 eligible gold days (1626-05-17, 1627-04-11) as having zero paragraph-axis records at all -- 'not investigated further' at the time -- distinct from the 12 structurally-abstaining days (axis coarser than K_e).
- **Decision:** Not a pipeline bug. resolutions_flat has zero rows for both calendar dates, so build_boundary_gold_paragraph_axis.py correctly emits zero axis records -- there is nothing to attach. Checked output/boundary_gold_sample.json directly: both days have k_e=1, k_f=0, flat_ids=[] -- the enriched record exists (162617mei.xml#0 / 162711ap.xml#0) but no flat/HTR resolution was ever matched to it. This is the same missing_htr structural ceiling already accepted as final for resolution_concordance_1626_1630 (2026-09-18 decision, 49.7% of the ledger has no HTR within +/-14 days), showing up here as a paragraph-axis gap instead. Since k_e=1 for both, they are trivial zero-boundary days with no segmentation signal to lose -- same category as the two k_e==1 zero-anchor days (1628-01-01, 1630-03-03) already fixed this iteration. No further action needed; do not re-investigate this as a new bug.
- **Reason:** Root-caused with data already on disk (resolutions_flat, boundary_gold_sample.json) -- no new tooling or heavy compute needed. Confirms the missing_htr ceiling is the same underlying cause surfacing in a second track, which is useful corroboration but not a new problem to solve, consistent with that track being closed as final.

## 2026-09-19: Add a proposal step to svz.py review; iterative tasks surface to strategy on completion

- **Context:** docs/APPROACH_OVERVIEW.md traced why whole branches (align_short_resolutions.py, the corpus-wide fuzzy scan) became orphans: work that is not registered is not measured, work that is not measured is not iterated. The 2026-09-19 session was the worked example -- svz.py review ran at session start and correctly flagged S4's boundary F1 as stagnant, but because it reported status without proposing a next step, the session still spent itself on the one branch that already had an evaluation harness, plus a long local-LLM infrastructure detour that advanced no track.
- **Decision:** Three changes. (1) Registered the orphans as real tracks: short-resolution-alignment (todo), fuzzy-surface-form-scan (inprogress), plus interior-cut-evaluation-harness (todo) -- a missing piece, not an orphan script, without which interior-cut work is structurally invisible to the tracked S4 metrics. (2) svz.py review now ends with a 'Proposed next step' section naming one recommended track with a reason and a goal, ranked by leverage (count of downstream tracks) then cheapness, never by momentum; unmeasurable tracks get a ranking bonus because they cannot be judged at all. Tasks gained optional next_action/goal/cost/blocks fields (svz.py update --next-action/--goal/--cost/--blocks), with next_action falling back to parsing 'Next action:' from existing notes. doctor validates blocks references and cost values. (3) Adopted control flow: finishing or terminating a subtask returns to strategy and RECOMPUTES the recommendation from the new state rather than resuming the previous queue -- because a completed subtask can change which track is highest-leverage. Termination must write back a metric and an outcome; descents get a budget whose breach is itself a trigger to surface early. Revert is the default, overridable by the user.
- **Reason:** The failure was never missing information -- the status board was accurate and was read. It was that reporting status leaves the prioritising to whoever is already deepest in a branch, and that person is the least able to notice they should be elsewhere. A recommendation with a stated reason and goal can be argued with; a status board can only be scrolled past. Ranking by leverage makes the argument explicit: the overlap-builder audit now surfaces first because it is upstream of 2 open tracks and would make 2 closed ones suspect if it fails, at cheap cost -- an ordering no amount of momentum would have produced.

## 2026-09-19: excel-overlap-builder-audit: literal-substring bug confirmed

- **Context:** place/org/per_overlap_1626_1630.xlsx builders were suspected of the same id-as-name/literal-substring bug class fixed in build_alignment_new.py by entity-resolution-fix. build_windowed_overlap.py already contains an unused --compare-legacy rebuild path (variant-lookup + tag_text fallback) for place/org; no equivalent existed for PER.
- **Decision:** Confirmed yes for all three. Place: build_windowed_overlap.py --window-days 0 --compare-legacy shows legacy canonical-only matching (9,428 rows) is a strict subset of the variant+tag_text-aware rebuild (10,693 rows, legacy_only=0, rebuilt_only=1,265, +13.4%). Org: legacy 1,363 rows vs rebuilt 1,418 (legacy_only=0, rebuilt_only=55, +4.0%). Per: build_per_overlap.py has no fuzzy/variant pass at all (exact substring + exact-word surname-token fallback only, verified by reading the source) -- directional cross-check against entity_surface_matches_1626_1630 finds 1,123 fuzzy-confirmed PER pairs across 991 resolutions entirely absent from per_overlap_1626_1630.xlsx (different candidate universe than per_overlap, so not a clean rate, but consistent with the same bug class). Also found in passing: build_windowed_overlap.py's institution-name lookup uses a separate unregistered instelling_info.json path rather than entity-resolution-fix's instituten_lookup_cleaned -- checked and the two are value-identical (0 diffs, same 56 rows), so no live discrepancy today, but it is a second untracked path to the same fact and should be collapsed onto one source.
- **Reason:** legacy_only=0 in both rebuild comparisons proves the fix is strictly additive (no regressions), so this is a safe, cheap swap rather than a re-litigation risk. Quantifying it (rather than leaving it as a suspicion) unblocks a decision on whether to adopt the rebuilt tables as canonical.

- **Context:** build_windowed_overlap.py --window-days 0 rebuild was verified strictly-additive on the confirmed key set (legacy_only=0, +1265 place / +55 org rows) 2026-09-19; versioned outputs (place_overlap_same_day_1626_1630.xlsx, org_overlap_same_day_1626_1630.xlsx) already existed on disk, no rebuild needed.

## 2026-09-20: S6: reframe segmentation as multi-channel anchor chaining at character coordinates

- **Context:** User proposed (1) reformulating the problem as DNA-style sequence alignment, (2) integrating ALL anchor contributions into one alignment comparison, (3) making sessions an anchor rather than a cutoff. Checked against docs/SEGMENTATION_TRANSFER.md section 6: NW-over-entity-sequences was already the design, and section 6.3 already specified that cut points land at CHARACTER positions and that the paragraph-vs-line granularity question 'dissolves'. The implementation drifted: s4_paragraph_axis_baseline.py:155-156 emits paragraph_stream_index as the output coordinate, with char_offset only as a phrase-snap byproduct (0 when no hit).
- **Decision:** Registered track s6-anchor-chain-alignment (todo, multi-session) with guide docs/steps/STEP_S6_anchor_chain_alignment.md. Measured the coordinate claim on all 50 gold days: 109/352 cut slots (31.0%, across 25 days) share a paragraph index; distinct reachable positions 280 -> 320 under (paragraph_stream_index, char_offset), hard recall ceiling 79.5% -> 90.9%, with all 32 residual unseparated slots having char_offset=null (annotation gap, not a coordinate limit). This REOPENS boundary_f1_tolerance0/tolerance2, closed 2026-09-18, on a new axis -- legitimately, because character coordinates are the 'finer-than-paragraph granularity' that closure required, and unlike the line-level track they need no new ground truth and no sessions_json extraction. Anchors are weighted per provenance GROUP (A tagger entity layer, B dictionary/fuzzy surface, C formulaic text, D structural/temporal), never flat, because group A's three channels share one tagger. Algorithm is colinear anchor chaining, not global NW and not an off-the-shelf bioinformatics library.
- **Reason:** The ceiling that closed those metrics was an implementation artifact of the output coordinate, not a property of the data -- and the character-level ground truth needed to prove it was already sitting in output/boundary_gold_sample.json (332/373 boundary records carry char_offset). Sequencing S6a (character-coordinate harness) first is non-negotiable: the interior-cut branch already demonstrated that work invisible to the tracked metrics does not accumulate.

## 2026-09-20: Correction: the paragraph-granularity ceiling is rigorous only at tolerance 0

- **Context:** The 2026-09-18 entry closing boundary_f1_tolerance0/tolerance2 stated that compute_boundary_prf 'can award at most one hyp match per distinct ref value', giving 'max reachable TP = count of distinct ref values', and read the 5-day subset result 'tol2 TP (24) reaches exactly 100% of it' as evidence the metric was saturated. The S6 guide written 2026-09-20 initially repeated that framing. Probed it directly against evaluation_harness.compute_boundary_prf.
- **Decision:** The claim holds at tolerance 0 only. At tol=0 a hypothesis must equal a reference exactly, and since predictions are strictly increasing, at most one can claim each distinct ref value -- a hard bound. At tol>0 it is not a property of the metric: against ref=[7,7,7], a CLUSTERED hypothesis [5,6,7] scores TP=3 at tolerance 2, while a spread [3,7,11] scores TP=1. The bound appeared to hold at tol2 only because the interpolate-then-snap predictor emits one cut per paragraph by construction and so cannot cluster. Therefore tol2 was NOT provably saturated on 2026-09-18; there was headroom the ceiling framing concealed. Corrected in docs/steps/STEP_S6_anchor_chain_alignment.md section 1(a); the tol0 figures (280/352 = 79.5% paragraph coords, 320/352 = 90.9% char coords) are unaffected and remain rigorous.
- **Reason:** The overstated version would have mis-set S6's expectations twice over: it understates how much tol2 headroom the current predictor is leaving on the table, and it credits the metric with a limitation that actually belongs to the predictor family. Both readings still argue for character coordinates -- placing several cuts inside one paragraph is exactly what they enable -- so the S6 direction is unchanged, but the stated reason had to be made accurate before the track is executed against it.

## 2026-09-20: S6a: character-axis ceiling is 313/352 (0.889), not 320/352 (0.909)

- **Context:** STEP_S6_anchor_chain_alignment.md section 1 states that moving to (paragraph_stream_index, char_offset) raises the tolerance-0 hard recall bound on the 50-day gold set from 280/352 (79.5%) to 320/352 (90.9%), and S6a's exit criterion is to record that ceiling as a metric.
- **Decision:** Record the ceiling as 313/352 = 0.889. Of 352 gold cut slots, 39 carry char_offset=null; those 39 fall into 7 distinct (date, paragraph_index) groups. The 320 figure counts those 7 groups as reachable positions alongside 313 genuinely distinct character positions (313 + 7 = 320). A slot with no offset has no character coordinate, so it is unreachable on this axis by construction.
- **Reason:** Verified by strict composition in scripts/s6a_char_axis_evaluation.py: all 313 offset-bearing cuts compose to 313 distinct positions (no collisions remain), 0 cuts are off-axis, and 0 cuts fall on axis-less days. A paragraph-start fallback for null offsets would recover exactly 320, but those 7 positions would each absorb several folded slots, so it buys reachability by reintroducing the folding the axis exists to remove. The character axis still beats paragraph coordinates by a real margin (0.795 -> 0.889); only the size of the gain changes, +9.4 points rather than +11.4.

## 2026-09-20: S6 Step 1: the placement model binds, not anchor supply

- **Context:** STEP_S6 section 2.3 proposes colinear anchor chaining to replace S4's entity-only NW. Chaining improves which anchors are trusted; it does not change what happens between chosen anchors, nor the coordinate the model emits. An oracle-anchor run is therefore an upper bound on what any anchor-selection improvement, chaining included, can reach.
- **Decision:** Do not build S6c's chaining DP on the current placement model. Handed a PERFECT gold-derived anchor set, interpolate_positions + snap_boundaries predicts only 5 of 19 scoreable gold days (coverage 0.263): 9 days abstain folded_anchors_non_monotone and 5 axis_shorter_than_k_e. On the 5 it can predict, micro F1 is 0.727 / 0.788 / 0.788 at tol 0 / 50 / 150 chars. Proceed instead with S6b (anchor harvest, useful under any algorithm) and a count-constrained segmentation DP that places exactly K_e-1 cuts at character granularity.
- **Reason:** 74% of days are unusable with perfect information, so anchor selection is not the binding constraint. Two structural causes, both untouched by a better search: interpolate_positions requires anchors strictly increasing in both coordinates, which genuinely-folded gold cuts violate; and the model emits (paragraph index, snap offset), so the 33 of 177 mid_paragraph gold cuts (18.6%) are largely unreachable at tight tolerance -- which is why oracle F1 caps at 0.788 rather than near 1.0. Measured by scripts/s6_oracle_anchor_diagnostic.py (18 tests) on the S6a character axis. CAVEAT: the all-density (5 days) and endpoints-density (11 days) rows cover different day sets -- endpoints covers more precisely because thinning to two anchors dodges the non-monotone check -- so the 0.788 vs 0.645 gap is not a density effect.

## 2026-09-20: S6b constraint baseline: gap counts flatter the problem; 19% of gaps hold 75% of the work

- **Context:** SEGMENTATION_TRANSFER.md section 7 argues that with ~1 tier-1 anchor every 2.3 rows, unanchored gaps are short (typically 1-2 resolutions) and therefore 'the residual problem is small and locally constrained'. Measured directly for the first time over a contiguous window (1626-01-01..1626-06-30) by scripts/s6b_known_point_ledger.py, which chains typed known points (session start/end sentinels, tier-1 anchors, gold boundaries) at character coordinates and counts the resolutions that must fall in each gap.
- **Decision:** The section 7 claim holds by gap count and fails by resolution count. Of 495 gaps: 263 are determined (0 free resolutions), 139 nearly determined (1-2), 93 open (>2). That is 81.2% determined-or-nearly BY GAP -- but those gaps hold only 179 of 723 unplaced resolutions (24.8%), while the 93 open gaps hold 544 (75.2%), spanning 397k characters and running as long as 21 resolutions. Report the resolution-weighted share alongside any gap-count share; the gap-count figure alone is misleading.
- **Reason:** A gap of size 0 contributes a row to the distribution but zero unplaced resolutions, so gap-count statistics are dominated by already-solved intervals. Only 389 of 1218 resolutions (31.9%) are pinned by a known point at all (366 tier-1 anchors, 41 gold boundaries across the 4 gold days in the window). Separately, 75 of the 181 days in the window have enriched resolutions but no paragraph axis at all, consistent with the accepted missing_htr ceiling. Effort should concentrate on the 93 long gaps rather than on the already-constrained majority.

- **Context:** The 2026-09-19 entry adopted the variant/tag_text-aware rebuild for place/org but left PER unscoped (`build_per_overlap.py` had no fuzzy pass). `build_per_overlap.py` was since extended to reuse `build_windowed_overlap.py`'s canonical/variant/tag_text framework plus a person-specific surname-token fallback pass, and run as `--window-days 0 --compare-legacy` against the real corpus.
- **Decision:** Swapped canonical `data/derived/per_overlap_1626_1630.xlsx` to the rebuilt content (7,959 rows: 2,678 canonical + 2,572 variant + 2,033 tag_text + 676 surname-token matches, 4,038/4,106 paragraph_ids resolved). On the `volgnr`+`paragraph_id`+`name` key, `legacy_only=0` (strict superset), `shared=7,544`, `rebuilt_only=415`. Legacy content backed up to `data/derived/legacy_backup/per_overlap_1626_1630.pre_rebuild_2026-09-21.xlsx`. Manifest description updated; `data_io.check` passes. Downstream consumers (`build_alignment_new.py`, `s4_paragraph_axis_baseline.py`, `s4_corpus_paragraph_predictions.py`) NOT yet re-run against the now-fully-swapped place/org/per overlap trio — that remains heavy compute left for the user.
- **Reason:** Same zero-regression bar as the 2026-09-19 place/org swap: every legacy-matched key is retained, plus 415 net-new rows from the variant/tag_text/surname-token passes the literal-substring builder missed. ## 2026-09-21: S6b anchor harvest: anchor supply is not the corpus-wide bottleneck

- **Context:** STEP_S6_anchor_chain_alignment.md's S6b row asks for an anchor harvest across all four provenance groups (A tagger entity layer, B dictionary/fuzzy surface, C formulaic text, D structural/temporal) and a measurement of "how far it closes the 815 insufficient_entity_anchors abstentions." Built `scripts/s6b_anchor_harvest.py` (9/9 tests) and ran it corpus-wide over 1626-1630: 247,871 anchor rows over 1,240 days-with-axis (group A 5,332 tier1 anchors/993 days; group B 18,406 `entity_surface_matches` + 189,764 `fuzzy_surface_scan` anchors/up to 1,238 days; group C 14,890 + 16,999 phrase-hit anchors/~1,231-1,233 days; group D 2,480 session sentinels).
- **Decision:** Of the 815 corpus-wide `insufficient_entity_anchors` abstentions, only 59 (7.2%) had zero group-A anchors at all; the other 756 (92.8%) already had at least one group-A anchor and abstained anyway, because `interpolate_positions` rejects them for monotonicity or axis-length reasons, not anchor scarcity. Of the 59 zero-anchor days, 57 (96.6%) gain a non-A anchor once groups B/C are harvested, but that is only 57/815 = 7.0% of all abstentions -- despite group B/C anchor volume being enormous relative to group A (`fuzzy_surface_scan` alone touches 162,307 distinct character positions across 1,238 of 1,240 days). Therefore: do not build S6c as a chaining step whose deliverable is resolving more group B/C anchors into `interpolate_positions`-compatible enriched-index pins -- that would leave 93% of abstentions untouched. Redirect S6c to a count-constrained segmentation DP that works directly inside each gap from `s6b_known_point_ledger.py`'s chain (947 open corpus-wide gaps hold 70.7% of unplaced resolutions per the 2026-09-20 constraint baseline), using B/C raw positions as soft in-gap placement evidence rather than as pre-resolved anchors.
- **Reason:** This corroborates the S6 Step 1 oracle-anchor diagnostic (2026-09-20) at full corpus scale rather than just the 19 scoreable gold days: handing the model a perfect anchor set there still only unlocked 5/19 days, because the placement model itself -- not anchor supply -- was shown to bind. The anchor-harvest run tests the same claim from the supply side (more anchors, still the old placement model) and gets the same answer (~93% unaffected), which is strong enough agreement across two independent measurements to redirect engineering effort rather than build the chaining step the original step guide proposed.

## 2026-09-21: Caveat on the S6b anchor-supply finding: measured on stale alignment/prediction inputs

- **Context:** The same-day "S6b anchor harvest: anchor supply is not the corpus-wide bottleneck" decision above rests on `alignment_1626_1630.parquet` (source of every group-A `tier1_entity_nw` anchor) and `s4_corpus_paragraph_predictions.jsonl` (source of the 815-day `insufficient_entity_anchors` list). Checked file mtimes while following up on `svz.py review`'s repeated recommendation of `adopt-windowed-overlap-rebuild`: `alignment_1626_1630.parquet` was last built 2026-08-28 and `s4_corpus_paragraph_predictions.jsonl` 2026-08-31 -- both before the place/org overlap rebuild (2026-09-19) and the PER overlap rebuild (2026-09-21) landed. Neither file has been regenerated against the now-fully-swapped overlap tables (+1,265 place / +55 org / +415 PER rows).
- **Decision:** Treat the 815 / 59 / 57 figures as **not yet final**. Re-run `build_alignment_new.py` and `s4_corpus_paragraph_predictions.py` against the swapped overlap tables (closing `adopt-windowed-overlap-rebuild`'s own open downstream-re-run action), then re-run `scripts/s6b_anchor_harvest.py` on the refreshed inputs and check whether the 92.8%-of-abstentions-already-have-a-group-A-anchor split still holds before treating it as load-bearing for the S6c redesign.
- **Reason:** The placement-model ceiling (`interpolate_positions`'s monotonicity/axis-length rejection) is a property of the algorithm, not the anchor count, so it is likely to survive a refresh -- but "likely" is not "measured," and this project's own discipline (docs/ITERATION_POLICY.md) is to verify rather than assume a claim still holds against current data. Cheap to check: both re-runs were already scoped as `adopt-windowed-overlap-rebuild`'s next action before this caveat was found.

## 2026-09-21: S6b anchor-supply finding re-confirmed on refreshed inputs -- caveat resolved

- **Context:** The 2026-09-21 caveat flagged that the 815/59/57 anchor-supply split was measured on alignment_1626_1630.parquet (built 2026-08-28) and s4_corpus_paragraph_predictions.jsonl (built 2026-08-31), both predating the place/org overlap rebuild (2026-09-19) and PER overlap rebuild (2026-09-21). Re-ran build_alignment_new.py (11:43) then scripts.s4_corpus_paragraph_predictions (11:44) against the swapped overlap tables, then scripts.s6b_anchor_harvest (11:57-12:28, parallelized+checkpointed, see scripts/s6b_anchor_harvest.py) on the refreshed inputs, full 1626-1630 corpus, 1240 days, 247914 anchor rows.
- **Decision:** The split holds and is slightly more lopsided than before: 777 insufficient_entity_anchors abstentions (down from 815), of which only 19 (2.4%, down from 59/7.2%) have zero group-A anchors, and 18 of those gain a non-A anchor from groups B/C (2.3% of all abstentions, down from 57/7.0%). 758/777 = 97.6% of abstentions (up from 92.8%) already had a group-A anchor and abstained anyway. Caveat resolved: do not build S6c as a chaining step that resolves more anchors for the existing interpolate_positions. The binding constraint is the placement model's monotonicity/axis-length rejection, confirmed on both the S6 Step 1 oracle diagnostic and now this anchor-supply measurement on fresh data.
- **Reason:** This project's discipline (docs/ITERATION_POLICY.md) is to verify a load-bearing claim against current data rather than assume a stale measurement still holds; the refresh was cheap (build_alignment_new.py + s4_corpus_paragraph_predictions + a newly-parallelized s6b_anchor_harvest, ~35 min total wall time) relative to the cost of building S6c on a wrong premise.

## 2026-09-21: S6c target metric rescoped: paragraph-level coverage, not character-exact tolerance

- **Context:** Investigated whether any downstream consumer of segmentation-transfer boundaries actually needs character-exact cut points, before committing to S6c's design. Findings: s4_resolution_concordance.py (the one real downstream assembly step, accepted 2026-09-18) treats paragraph attribution as explicitly optional best-effort, not a required status input (docs/CANDIDATE_SCORING_AND_CONCORDANCE.md:327-338). build_alignment_new.py, the live entity-matching pipeline, operates entirely on paragraph_id -> resolution_id and never reads character offsets. NER training pairs (build_loc_training_pairs.py / build_per_training_pairs.py) take their character offsets from the separate LOC-/PER-annotations.json layer, unrelated to segmentation-transfer output. resolution_concordance_1626_1630 currently has zero downstream readers. The char-axis (S6/S6a/S6b) push itself was motivated by defeating a self-imposed plateau on boundary_f1_tolerance0 (docs/DECISIONS.md 2026-09-20), not by a named consumer's requirement.
- **Decision:** S6c should target coverage and paragraph-level (lumped) accuracy, not tol0/tol50 character-exact placement. This also sidesteps the risk flagged earlier: S6c's originally-scoped in-gap placement (using B/C anchors as soft evidence to place a precise character cut) would likely have inherited s4_bundled_split_poc.py's already-measured weak precision (0-15% recall at tol50). Scoped to paragraph-level placement instead, S6c's job narrows to fixing interpolate_positions's over-eager abstention (only 5/19 days predicted even with oracle-perfect anchors, per the S6 Step 1 diagnostic) while keeping today's paragraph-level placement quality (0.828 lumped) -- a much easier, better-evidenced target.
- **Reason:** User confirmed the suspicion that full resolution-boundary precision is not needed; verified against actual code/doc citations rather than assumed. Keeps S6c scoped to the part of the problem (abstention/coverage) the project has strong positive evidence it can fix, instead of the part (sub-paragraph character placement) it has strong negative evidence against.

## 2026-09-21: adopt-windowed-overlap-rebuild: downstream re-run checked, headline tier stats barely move -- closing the track

- **Context:** The track's own goal (state.json) was 'canonical overlap tables include the variant/fuzzy recovery, and downstream tier/confidence stats reflect it (or a measured reason not to adopt)'. The place/org swap (2026-09-19) and PER swap (2026-09-21) were both done, and build_alignment_new.py / s4_corpus_paragraph_predictions.py were re-run against them on 2026-09-21 (11:43-11:44) as part of closing the S6b anchor-supply caveat -- but nobody had checked build_alignment_new.py's own headline output (confidence_tier distribution) against the pre-swap numbers documented in PLAN.md's 'Tiered Milestone Alignment' section.
- **Decision:** Loaded the current alignment_1626_1630.parquet (13342 rows, same total as before the swap) and compared confidence_tier counts to the pre-swap figures already in PLAN.md: tier1_anchor 5725->5730 (42.9%->42.9%), tier2_merged_page+tier2_interpolated+tier2_adjacent 4734->4708 (35.5%->35.3%), tier3_head_template+tier3_tail 2883->2904 (21.6%->21.8%). Largest single-tier delta is 26 rows, 0.19 percentage points. Mark adopt-windowed-overlap-rebuild done: both halves (place/org rebuild, PER rebuild) were adopted, the downstream re-run happened, and the measured reason for the near-flat headline stats is now on record.
- **Reason:** This corroborates the S6b anchor-supply finding from the same refresh (insufficient_entity_anchors abstentions 815->777) rather than contradicting it: the +1265/+55/+415 recovered rows are a small fraction of the ~13.3k alignment pairs and the ~18.7k entity_surface_matches corpus, so they nudge tier composition without materially reshaping it. The overlap-table swap was still worth doing (zero-regression, net-new true positives per the 2026-09-19/09-21 swap entries) -- it just isn't the lever that moves S4/S6 headline metrics, consistent with S6b's conclusion that anchor supply is not the binding constraint there either.

## 2026-09-21: S6c: count-constrained segmentation DP built and wired in

- **Context:** S6c was rescoped 2026-09-21 to fix interpolate_positions's over-eager abstention (only 5/19 oracle-anchor days predicted) rather than resolve more anchors for it. Two failure modes named by the oracle diagnostic: folded_anchors_non_monotone (9/19) and axis_shorter_than_k_e (5/19), both cases lumped paragraph-level scoring already tolerates.
- **Decision:** Built scripts/s6c_gap_segmentation.py (segment_gap/segment_day, 11 tests) and swapped it in for interpolate_positions in both scripts/s4_paragraph_axis_baseline.py and scripts/s4_corpus_paragraph_predictions.py. It never abstains: out-of-order anchors are clipped forward into a non-decreasing backbone anchored at session start/end sentinels, and gaps narrower than their resolution count get repeated paragraph assignments instead of aborting the day. Group-C phrase hits feed it as soft in-gap evidence at gold-day scale; group B and corpus-scale phrase evidence are deferred (would need a ~30min parallel FuzzyPhraseSearcher pass like s6b_anchor_harvest.py). interpolate_positions itself is untouched -- s6_oracle_anchor_diagnostic.py still needs the unmodified model.
- **Reason:** Re-ran the 50-gold-day pipeline (evaluate_s4_paragraph_axis.py, s6a_char_axis_evaluation.py): predicted-day coverage 9/21 -> 19/21 (eligible) and 7/19 -> 19/19 (scoreable, S6a's denominator) -- full coverage of every day with an axis. Lumped paragraph-level F1 held essentially flat (0.828 -> 0.826 at tol50/150), and strict character F1 was roughly flat at tol50 (0.500 -> 0.503) despite predicting on nearly 3x as many days, including the harder ones the old model used to refuse. This is the coverage win S6c's rescoped target asked for, achieved without a corpus-wide run.

## 2026-09-21: S6c corpus-wide re-run: insufficient_entity_anchors eliminated

- **Context:** Previous session validated S6c's count-constrained segmentation DP only on the 50 gold days (coverage 9/21 -> 19/21). The corpus-wide question left open: does the same abstention-free behavior hold over the full 1626-1630 window, where the last measured insufficient_entity_anchors count was 777 (of ~1594 enriched-dated days, 535 structurally missing_htr).
- **Decision:** User ran uv run python -m scripts.s4_corpus_paragraph_predictions (no code changes since the prior session). Result: 1059 predicted / 535 abstained, and the abstained set is now missing_htr only -- insufficient_entity_anchors is exactly 0 (was 777). 1059 = 282 (pre-existing predictions) + 777 (every previously-abstained day now predicts), an exact match confirming the mechanism, not a coincidence.
- **Reason:** This is a decisive corpus-scale confirmation of the gold-day result: 100% of days with any paragraph axis now receive a prediction. Quality caveat quantified directly from the output (no gold labels exist corpus-wide, so this substitutes for an F1 check): of 1059 predicted days, 302 are clean (no repeated paragraph index), 516 have a minor 2-3-way collapse, 114 have 4-6, and 79 (7.5%) have 7+ resolutions collapsed onto one paragraph (max 24) -- these are low-localization coverage wins, not segmentation, an expected and disclosed consequence of allowing repeats rather than a bug. S6c's rescoped exit criterion (coverage + gold-day lumped F1, not char precision) is met; group-B evidence and corpus-scale phrase-hit wiring remain deferred, scoped as the next increment only if the severe-collapse share needs improving.

## 2026-09-21: S6d pivoted: wire corpus predictor to resolution_concordance_1626_1630 instead of building session-as-anchor chaining from scratch

- **Context:** STEP_S6_anchor_chain_alignment.md scoped S6d as 'drop the per-day partition; run per inventory with session starts as group-D anchors' to recover the 535 missing_htr days and 7 gold cross-day-shift days. Before building that, found a separate, more mature, already-'accepted final' track (S4a-g: session_date_status_1626_1630 -> s4_day_status_resolution -> resolution_concordance_1626_1630) that already resolves session/day mapping per enriched resolution, including confident entity-overlap candidate scoring over ambiguous -1/+1/? ledger rows -- exactly the orphan pattern STEP_S6 section 1b warned about: multiple resolution mechanisms built and not talking to each other. s4_corpus_paragraph_predictions.py (the thing S6c just fixed) was grouping paragraphs by raw calendar date only and never consulted it.
- **Decision:** Wired scripts/s4_corpus_paragraph_predictions.py to fall back to resolution_concordance_1626_1630's resolved_session_id (day_status == resolved_auto) when a date has no same-calendar-day axis, instead of building new session-as-anchor alignment/chaining logic. Two new pure functions (session_of, axis_for_date, 4 new tests) plus a resolved_sessions() loader. Did NOT auto-select ledger -1/+1 candidates directly (rejected as a separate option): docs/SESSION_DATE_MAPPING_REVIEW.md already decided those need human review with a mandatory note, and 95% of unique -1/+1 candidates turned out to also be their true neighbor day's own T/E match, confirming that needs a human looking at the actual text, not a blanket policy.
- **Reason:** Re-ran uv run python -m scripts.s4_corpus_paragraph_predictions (no other changes): predicted 1059 -> 1140, missing_htr abstentions 535 -> 454, exactly 81 days recovered for free from work already done and already accepted by a separate track. Full test suite (354 passed) and data_io.check are clean. This is a data-source wiring fix, not new segmentation logic -- consistent with the 'replace, don't accrete' guardrail. Remaining gaps are out of scope for this session: the 365 -1/+1 candidates need the pending human-review workflow (docs/SESSION_DATE_MAPPING_REVIEW.md), and the 7 gold cross-day-shift days (trailing contaminated text on a day that DOES have its own axis) are a different failure mode this wiring does not touch.

## 2026-09-21: S4f review surfaced resolutions_flat session-numbering drift -- bigger than the -1/+1 queue itself

- **Context:** Took the S6d handoff's option (a): scoped build_session_date_mapping_review_ui.py to just the -1/+1 rows (--status filter, 365 rows) and excluded 64 nihil-actum rows (drop_nihil_actum_rows, already auto-resolved ahead of human review by s4_day_status_resolution.py's precedence), leaving 301. User hand-reviewed ~10 and exported decisions (data/import/s4_session_date_mapping_decisions.json). 4/9 approvals confirmed a real mid-session-merge signal -- a "President de Heer X, Present de <weekday> den <date>" session-start formula appearing partway through one HTR-parsed session block, meaning two real sessions got concatenated under one session id. A new lenient diagnostic (scripts/s4_session_start_scan.py, prefix-stem regex on presid/presen) reproduced this at scale: 210 candidate mid-text starts across 167/1511 sessions (11.1%). But 3/9 no-match decisions cited true dates 2-3 days outside the ledger's +/-1-day candidate window, found via app.goetgevonden.nl's per-resolution URNs. Investigating those dates led to pulling raw sessions_json_source JSON directly (new warm-tier dataset s4_session_date_region_scan, 51677 text_regions across all 1690 sessions in the ledger's 6 covered inventories, 179 more than resolutions_flat holds any row for at all).
- **Decision:** The app.goetgevonden.nl dates turned out not to be independent ground truth -- they matched resolutions_flat's own per-session date exactly wherever checked. The real finding came from matching a 150-char content fingerprint from each resolutions_flat session against its own inventory's raw sessions in sessions_json_source: 1128/1511 (75%) matched a unique raw session, 0 ambiguous, 383 unmatched. resolutions_flat's session numbering has drifted from the current archive, confirmed directly (e.g. resolutions_flat's session-3185-num-25-resolution-1 text matches raw session-3185-num-26 verbatim; num-26's matches raw num-27). Drift shape varies by inventory: 3185/3186/3189 accumulate a monotonic +1-at-a-time staircase (reaching +9/+13/+8 by year end), 3187 is mild (flat at 0 for ~240 sessions, then +1, +2), 3188 is bidirectional (starts at -2, crosses to +8), and 4562 oscillates between 0/+1/+2 dozens of times rather than accumulating -- a qualitatively different, unexplained case. Every step changes by exactly +/-1, consistent with individual sessions being inserted or removed one at a time as the archive was re-segmented since resolutions_flat was built.
- **Reason:** A real share of -1/+1 (and likely A/X/N) ledger rows reflect this numbering drift rather than genuine nearby-day ambiguity -- the offered candidate session id is simply mislabeled relative to its actual content, so no amount of human review of the current queue framing fixes that. Fixing the ledger itself needs a per-inventory realignment against sessions_json_source, out of scope for continuing the -1/+1 review as currently framed. Chose not to keep manually reviewing the 301-row queue once this was clear (~10 rows were enough to surface and validate the pattern). Next step scoped as two new Group-D channels in scripts/s6b_anchor_harvest.py per STEP_S6 section 2.2, not yet built: session_day_find (direct date-phrase hits from raw text_regions, self-sufficient across all 1690 sessions, catches both false-negatives and false-positives already found in the upstream nlc_classifier's own "date" text_region_class tag) and session_date_verified (the content-fingerprint correspondence needed specifically to know what resolutions_flat currently mislabels a piece of content as). Open design question before either is a quick bolt-on: s6b's char_position axis is built from resolutions_flat's own (possibly drifted) text, so the new channels must anchor on content or archival provenance (scan_id/page_id/line_id), not the session-number label. The 1128 confirmed offset matches are only in scratch (/tmp/scope_results.json), not yet persisted to a manifest dataset.

## 2026-09-21: S6b: session-fingerprint match persisted as a registered dataset

- **Context:** PLAN.md's 2026-09-21 S4f session left the content-fingerprint correspondence between resolutions_flat session ids and raw sessions_json_source sessions (needed for STEP_S6 section 2.2's session_date_verified anchor channel) only in unregistered scratch (/tmp/scope_results.json, 1128/1511 matched via an undocumented method), explicitly flagged as needing regeneration/proper persistence before the channel work starts.
- **Decision:** Built scripts/s6b_session_fingerprint_match.py (9 tests) and registered s6b_session_fingerprint_match in data_manifest.toml. Method: 150-char normalized fingerprint of each resolutions_flat session's resolution text, matched by substring containment (not exact-prefix equality) against same-inventory raw sessions' concatenated 'para'-class region text from s4_session_date_region_scan. Exact-prefix matching found only 838/1511 (55.5%); substring containment finds 1059/1511 (70.1%) with 0 ambiguous matches, closer to and more principled than the scratch figure. Channel construction (session_day_find, session_date_verified) itself is not built this session -- this closes only the 'persist the fingerprint data' prerequisite.
- **Reason:** resolutions_flat's own paragraph splitting does not align 1:1 with raw para region boundaries (e.g. an attendance-like line mistagged 'para' gets spliced out of the flat text), so a flat session's real content can start partway into a raw session's concatenated text -- exact-prefix equality systematically undercounts genuine matches for this reason, not because the correspondence doesn't exist. data_io.check and the full test suite (363 passed) are clean.

## 2026-09-21: S6b: session_day_find channel built with FuzzyPhraseSearcher, not the stem regex -- and validated before wiring in

- **Context:** Built the first of the two Group-D channels the S4f/fingerprint-match sessions scoped: `session_day_find`, the internal "President de Heer X, Present de <weekday> den <date>" session-start formula found past a day's own opening (signalling a merged HTR session block). The obvious approach was to port `s4_session_start_scan.py`'s stem regex (`pr[ae]{1,2}sid\w*` / `pr[ae]{1,2}sent\w*`) as-is; queried instead whether `FuzzyPhraseSearcher` (already used for every other phrase channel in this file) would do better, since a literal regex can't absorb HTR character noise inside a correctly-spelled stem.
- **Decision:** Built `phrase_hits`/`session_day_find_rows` in `scripts/s6b_anchor_harvest.py` using `FuzzyPhraseSearcher` over two phrase lists grounded in the corpus itself (frequency scan over `paragraph_axis_1626_1630`): all 8 forms covering ~98% of "presid*" occurrences, and a **narrowed** present-family list (`present`, `presentibus`, `praesentibus`, `presentie`, `presente`) -- checked directly what co-occurs with "presid*" in a 60-char window (237/830 occurrences do) and found the corpus-wide-frequent `presentatie`/`presenterende`/`presenteren`/`presenteert` (generic "presentation"/attendance usage) appear there **zero times**; including them would only add noise. Wired as a new Group-D channel (weight 4.0, between tier1's 3.0 and the session-boundary sentinel's 5.0).
- **Reason:** Two bugs found and fixed before trusting any number. (1) `FuzzyPhraseSearcher`'s `ignorecase` defaults to `False` -- against real corpus text (sentence-initial "Preside et Presentibus...") this undercounted the regex baseline (210 hits/167 sessions on the same session-text universe) by 4x (54/45) until set to `True`; this also affects the existing `s2_anchor_phrases`/`s4_opening_phrase_candidates` channels' shared `PHRASE_SEARCH_CONFIG`, left unchanged here since their metrics predate this finding -- separate follow-up, not folded in silently. (2) At the existing 0.85 threshold, ignorecase-fixed, hit count jumped to 576/365 -- inspecting sampled snippets showed most were false positives: single short words like "present" (7 chars) fuzzy-match unrelated nearby substrings often enough that a generic "de heer President heeft geproponeerde..." mention got spuriously paired with noise and counted as a formula hit. Raising the threshold to 0.95 (still `ignorecase: True`) dropped the count to 154/130 with 25/25 sampled hits genuine (spot-checked twice, different random samples) -- lower recall than the regex baseline (unvalidated for precision) but real. **Scope note:** unlike STEP_S6's original wording ("self-sufficient: works on all 1,690 raw sessions"), this channel operates over `paragraph_axis_1626_1630` like the rest of the harvest, i.e. still resolutions_flat-derived, not raw `sessions_json_source` text_regions -- it does not yet reach the 179 sessions with zero resolutions_flat rows. `session_date_verified` (the other planned Group-D channel) is not built. 17/17 new+existing tests pass, full suite (367 passed) and `data_io.check` clean; not yet run corpus-wide (main()'s ~32-minute parallelized pass), only validated against the same session-text universe `s4_session_start_scan.py` used.

## 2026-09-21: session_day_find corpus-wide run -- smallest channel by volume, supply-gain proxy now degenerate

- **Context:** User ran the corpus-wide harvest (`uv run python -m scripts.s6b_anchor_harvest`, full 1626-1630 window) with `session_day_find` wired in, no other code changes.
- **Decision:** No code change; recording the corpus-scale reading. `session_day_find`: 203 anchors / 162 of 1,240 days-with-axis (13.1%), 203 distinct positions -- smallest of all six non-sentinel channels by anchor count (group A `tier1_entity_nw` alone has 5,375 anchors/995 days; group B's `fuzzy_surface_scan` has 189,764). `insufficient_entity_anchors` supply-gain proxy printed 0/0/0.
- **Reason:** The 0/0/0 proxy is degenerate, not a finding -- S6c (2026-09-21, above) already eliminated the `insufficient_entity_anchors` abstention category corpus-wide (777 → 0), so there is no abstained-day set left for the proxy to check gains against; it would read 0/0/0 regardless of what any new channel found. `session_day_find`'s real signal is its low density (162/1,240 = 13.1% of days touched) and validated precision (154/130 on the same session-text universe, 25/25 sampled genuine) -- a sparse, high-confidence anchor, not a coverage lever. Consistent with expectations: internal session-merge formulas are rare events (11.1% of raw sessions per `s4_session_start_scan.py`), not a per-day-typical signal like tier1 entities or phrase openings.

## 2026-09-21: S6b: session_day_find channel built with FuzzyPhraseSearcher, validated before wiring in

- **Context:** Built the first of two planned Group-D channels (STEP_S6 sec 2.2): an internal president/present session-start formula found mid-axis, signalling a merged HTR session block. Chose FuzzyPhraseSearcher over porting s4_session_start_scan.py's stem regex, since the project's own guardrails favor reusing the existing phrase-search tool over a bespoke pattern.
- **Decision:** Wired phrase_hits/session_day_find_rows in scripts/s6b_anchor_harvest.py using FuzzyPhraseSearcher with ignorecase=True and threshold 0.95 (its own SESSION_START_PHRASE_SEARCH_CONFIG, not the shared PHRASE_SEARCH_CONFIG), and a present-phrase list narrowed to forms empirically confirmed to co-occur with presid* in a 60-char window.
- **Reason:** Two bugs found before trusting any count: (1) FuzzyPhraseSearcher's ignorecase defaults False, undercounting the s4_session_start_scan.py regex baseline (210/167) by 4x until fixed; (2) at the default 0.85 threshold, short words like 'present' fuzzy-match unrelated substrings, producing mostly false positives (spot-checked). At 0.95, 25/25 sampled hits were genuine; final count 154/130, lower recall than the unvalidated regex baseline but real. Scope caveat: still resolutions_flat-derived via paragraph_axis_1626_1630, not raw sessions_json_source, so it does not reach the 179 sessions with zero resolutions_flat rows. 17/17 tests in the file, full suite 367 passed, data_io.check clean.

## 2026-09-21: S6c: corpus-scale group-C phrase-hit wiring tested on gold days, measured negative, not wired in

- **Context:** Scoped wiring s4_opening_phrase_candidates evidence into s4_corpus_paragraph_predictions.py's segment_day call (the gap S6c's own docstring named as deferred, not the full ~32-min corpus-wide run). Refactored scripts/s4_paragraph_axis_baseline.py's phrase_hits(axis, phrases) to phrase_hits(axis, searcher) so a searcher can be built once and reused, matching s6b_anchor_harvest.py's pattern -- verified behavior-preserving by diffing s4_paragraph_axis_predictions.jsonl byte-for-byte before/after (identical). Before wiring the corpus predictor's main() to build a searcher and running the expensive full corpus pass, scored the change cheaply on just the 19/21 scoreable gold days using the corpus predictor's own predict()/grouped_enriched()/axis_for_date() codepath (not the separate gold-day baseline pipeline), reusing evaluate_s4_paragraph_axis.py's evaluate_segmentation_session/summarize_segmentation_evaluations harness.
- **Decision:** Did not wire phrase-hit evidence into the corpus predictor. Reverted the predict()/main() changes back to the unmodified segment_day(...) call with no position_scores, keeping only the phrase_hits refactor in s4_paragraph_axis_baseline.py (a pure, verified-identical efficiency change, independently useful).
- **Reason:** Measured net negative on the primary metric: tol0 F1 0.644 (no phrase evidence) -> 0.622 (with phrase evidence, via segment_day's position_scores bonus), same 19/21 coverage both ways. tol1/tol2 saw only marginal gains (+0.006 each) and mean Pk/WindowDiff got worse (0.285 -> 0.292). Also tested adding snap_boundaries (the gold-day pipeline's second phrase-evidence stage, which the corpus predictor never had) on top of the DP bonus -- produced IDENTICAL numbers, confirming this is a real result and not an artifact of a half-ported mechanism. Net effect: the phrase bonus sometimes pulls segment_gap's chosen position away from the correct one when several resolutions share a gap, trading exact hits for near-misses. Full test suite and data_io.check remain clean; no corpus-wide run was needed to reach this answer, saving the ~32-minute job for a result that would not have justified it.

## 2026-09-21: Demote tolerance metrics in favor of concordance and collapse counts

- **Context:** Character-level tolerance metrics (tol0/tol150) punish OCR noise/spacing and mask multi-resolution paragraph collapsing.
- **Decision:** Demote tol0/tol150 character metrics; adopt paragraph-level concordance and max-collapse count per session as primary drivers.
- **Reason:** Tolerance windows treat discrete paragraphs as continuous space and penalize semantically valid formulaic phrase snaps.

## 2026-09-22: Backfill acceptance-criteria decision (separated definition, ceiling, targets table)

- **Context:** PLAN.md's canonical-goal block (PLAN.md:17) cites 'docs/DECISIONS.md, 2026-09-22' as the source of the separated/ceiling/targets definition, but no such entry existed -- the last entry was 2026-09-21. Discovered while consolidating docs/STATE.md's metrics into docs/METRICS.md.
- **Decision:** Record here, as stated in PLAN.md: a resolution is separated iff its start paragraph is shared with no other resolution on its date and its extent is <=3 paragraphs. Paragraph-level ceiling: 11,644/19,120 = 60.9%. Targets against that ceiling: Global primary >=50% (>=5,822), Global stretch >=75% (>=8,733), Global spans >=6 qualifying spans totalling >=180 days, Local: any inventory-year reaching >=50% of its own ceiling. Baseline: 1,961 = 16.8% of ceiling.
- **Reason:** These numbers were manually derived and cited as a decision that was never logged. No script in the repo reproduces the literal figures (11644, 19120, 1961) -- flagged as an open gap in docs/METRICS.md rather than silently left uncited.

## 2026-09-22: Adopt channel-based metric tiers (O/P/A-E/V/X) in docs/METRICS.md

- **Context:** docs/STATE.md's ~59 metric lines were a flat, ungrouped list spanning unrelated tracks (S1 diagnostics, S4 boundary F1, HOE classifier, S6 anchor work, resolution-concordance completeness). User asked whether metrics form a tiered sequence; none existed -- the closest was PLAN.md's acceptance-criteria table, which orders targets, not the other ~46 diagnostics. The project already names anchor-signal channels A-D inline in PLAN.md's S6b session notes (PLAN.md:538-561); user chose to organize the metric tiers by those channels rather than an abstract distance-from-goal scheme.
- **Decision:** Created docs/METRICS.md defining Tier O (outcome/acceptance criteria, sourced from PLAN.md), Tier P (placement/model, cross-channel), Tier A-D (one per anchor channel; D also covers session-boundary/concordance completeness), Tier E (short-resolution anchors via align_short_resolutions.py, currently empty pending integration into scripts/s6b_anchor_harvest.py), Tier V (validation/consistency invariants -- pass/fail, not coverage), and Tier X (exploratory/historical readings that justified a past decision but are not ongoing tracked signals). Mapped every existing docs/STATE.md metric line into exactly one tier. Also specified four not-yet-computed proposals, all deferred to a following step: a Tier-O consecutive-day span table implementing the existing but never-measured 'Global, spans' criterion; a Tier-D per-day K_e drift-comparison diagnostic; a Tier-V nihil-actum invariant (a nihil_actum day must have zero attributed resolutions); and a Tier-P bottleneck diagnostic that reruns the S6 Step-1 oracle technique to decide whether anchor supply (A-E) or the placement algorithm (P) is the next worthwhile investment -- explicitly scoped as an extension of scripts/svz.py review and docs/ITERATION_POLICY.md, not a new orchestrator or DP solver.
- **Reason:** A flat metric list could not answer whether results are tiered, how they relate, or whether anything is missing. Channel-based grouping keeps each metric next to the thing that would move it, and matches the project's own aDNA framing (PLAN.md:11-15): Tiers A-D as anchor/seed classes, Tier P as the aligner whose own resolving-power limit is distinct from input quality, Tier O's spans as a coverage-breadth statistic, Tier V as a negative-control check. The bottleneck diagnostic stays a decision aid feeding the existing review mechanism rather than a parallel system, consistent with docs/ITERATION_POLICY.md's 'advisors and editors, not operators' convention. Only documentation changed this session; the four proposals are next-step work.

## 2026-09-22: Tier V day series parents Tier D K_e drift

- **Context:** docs/METRICS.md specified Tier V (nihil-actum invariant) and Tier D (K_e drift) as separate deferred proposals; state.json had V->O->D. User asked to fill V then D and noted D benefits from V.
- **Decision:** V emits the shared per-day K_e series (k_e_signal=0 on nihil days, invariant_ok gate). D consumes that series only — never rebuilds K_e from concordance. Nihil days are never drift candidates; V violations block those dates. Tier O spans table (still open) should reuse the same V parent.
- **Reason:** Keeps the negative-control (V) upstream of any drift/span reading so a failed invariant cannot silently inflate Tier D/O numbers; k_e_signal avoids treating the 1-row Nihil Actum formula as ordinary low-K_e work.

## 2026-09-22: Severe-collapse spot-check: missing text vs axis selection

- **Context:** User hypothesized that max>=7 pile-ups may reflect missing HTR rather than DP failure. Spot-checked P=1 pigeonhole days against concordance + paragraph_axis; quantified all 92.
- **Decision:** Three strata: (1) richer_session_ignored=9 — axis_for_date returns thin same-calendar stubs when concordance already maps to a richer session on a neighboring date (e.g. 1627-07-10: calendar P=1 vs session-3186-num-117 P=31 on 1627-07-12); fix is prefer concordance session when sess_P > same_P. (2) genuinely_thin_session=15 — both calendar and resolved session are P<=1, often inventory 4562 single blobs (hundreds–3k chars for K_e 10–15); real truncated/unsplittable HTR, not a selector bug. (3) thin_axis_no_better_session=56 — short axis with no richer session available. Do not treat all severe-collapse as missing text; next cheap code fix is (1).
- **Reason:** Direct evidence from axis + concordance for the worst P=1 days; 8/32 pigeonhole_forced are the selector bug, 15/32 are genuinely thin.

## 2026-09-22: axis_for_date must not treat non-empty same_day as a day cutoff

- **Context:** Current axis_for_date returns same-calendar-day paragraphs whenever the list is non-empty, falling back to concordance only if empty. Spot-check showed 9 collapse days where a thin calendar stub beat a richer neighbor-dated resolved session. User correction: that gate is wrong by any standard unless we have a proven day cutoff — calendar presence is not proof the sitting belongs on that date.
- **Decision:** Do not keep the 'if same_day: return same_day' rule. Prefer the concordance-resolved session whenever day_status is resolved_auto (or another proven cutoff such as session_date_verified / ledger T), and only use same-calendar-day axis when that cutoff says the calendar date is trusted OR when no resolved session exists. The weaker 'prefer whichever has more paragraphs' heuristic is a stopgap at best, not the acceptance criterion — richness is a symptom of the bug, not the authority relation.
- **Reason:** Concordance is the accepted day-mapping track; non-empty same-day HTR is not a verified date assignment under known session/date drift.

Relaxed merge validation so defer/no_match may carry a UI-highlighted selected_session_id (still nulled in output).

## 2026-09-23: axis_for_date fixed and remeasured

- **Context:** Implemented the 2026-09-22 `axis_for_date` decision: `scripts/s4_corpus_paragraph_predictions.py` now prefers the concordance-resolved session (`day_status == "resolved_auto"`) over a non-empty same-calendar-day stub, falling back to the same-day axis only when no resolved session is recorded or its axis is empty. 6 tests updated/added (`tests/test_s4_corpus_paragraph_predictions.py`, 8/8 passing); full suite 449 passed.
- **Decision:** Re-ran the full pipeline in sequence — `s4_corpus_paragraph_predictions` → `metrics_span_gap_map` → `s6c_severe_collapse_autopsy` — and accept the new numbers as current. Coverage unchanged (1,140 predicted / 454 `missing_htr` abstained — this fix changes *which* axis a day uses, not *whether* one is found). Of 1,140 predicted days, 1,057 now route through the resolved-session path instead of the same-day one, but only **77** actually change axis content (the other 980 resolve to the same underlying session either way — reported separately so the headline number isn't inflated by cosmetic routing changes). Severe-collapse days **92 → 83** (max pile-up 25 → 16); breaking gaps containing a collapse day **43 → 40** (25.15% → 23.39%, dropping `severe_collapse_implicated` from **True to False** against the 0.25 threshold — reversing the 2026-09-22 "reopen" call from the pre-fix numbers).
