Global-spans bridge-ceiling reasoning (longest 12 calendar days after bridging `weak_separation`) is unaffected — re-ran `metrics_span_gap_map` and it still reports 0 spans@30 under all interrupter classes.

## 2026-09-23: resolution_concordance_1626_1630 was stale, gating the entire Tier O separation baseline

- **Context:** Ran a cheap weak_separation-headroom diagnostic (min(1, paragraph_count/k_e_signal) structural ceiling per day) to decide whether densifying weak_separation needs a placement-quality fix (S6e) or a finer axis. Result: 91.4% of weak_separation days were headroom_available but median separated_share on those days was exactly 0.0 even where paragraph_count==k_e_signal (ceiling=1.0) -- implausible for a placement-quality gap alone. Traced to resolution_concordance_1626_1630's own provenance: created 2026-09-17T15:10, three predictor rebuilds behind s4_corpus_paragraph_predictions (S6c DP eliminating 777 insufficient_entity_anchors abstentions on 2026-09-21, the concordance-fallback wiring same day, and the axis_for_date fix on 2026-09-23). Only 113/11513 resolutions on sampled headroom dates had paragraph attribution; the rest showed paragraph_prediction_status=abstained even though the live predictions.jsonl record for that date was status=predicted.
- **Decision:** Re-ran scripts/s4_resolution_concordance.py (its own docstring: pure assembly, no new matching/alignment logic; 26s) then the full Tier O chain (metrics_nihil_actum_invariant -> metrics_separation_span_table -> metrics_span_gap_map -> s6c_severe_collapse_autopsy -> metrics_weak_separation_headroom_diagnostic). Rows with paragraph attribution jumped from a small fraction to all 13354 resolved_auto rows. PLAN.md's Global-primary headline moved 1961/11644=16.8% -> 8015/11644=68.8%, clearing the >=50% primary criterion (stretch >=75% not yet, gap ~718). Solid days 221/1594 -> 693/1594. Global spans@30 gap=2: 0 spans -> 4 spans/177 days (criterion needs >=6 spans/>=180 days, so close but not met). severe_collapse_implicated stays False. No new algorithm/modeling work was involved -- this closes an orphaned-track staleness gap of the same shape flagged repeatedly this project (STEP_S6 section 1(b)).
- **Reason:** The headroom diagnostic's own near-zero separated_share on ceiling=1.0 days was too implausible to be a genuine placement-quality signal, so it was checked against its data source before being trusted or used to scope S6e. docs/CANDIDATE_SCORING_AND_CONCORDANCE.md's own design intends paragraph attribution to track s4_corpus_paragraph_predictions live; nothing in that doc says concordance should be a frozen snapshot, so refreshing it is a data-hygiene fix, not a policy change.

## 2026-09-23: Group-B position_scores wiring measured negative, not wired into live predictor

- **Context:** PLAN.md 2026-09-23 scoped wiring entity_surface_matches_1626_1630 into segment_day's position_scores (same mechanism as the already-negative Group-C phrase hits), tested cheaply on the 19/21 scoreable gold days through s4_corpus_paragraph_predictions.predict()'s own codepath before any corpus-wide rerun. Metric corrected per user instruction this session: scored at PARAGRAPH tol0/1/2 (docs/DECISIONS.md 2026-09-21 'S6c target metric rescoped'), not character-offset tolerance.
- **Decision:** Do not wire Group B into predict()'s live position_scores. Baseline (no evidence) tol0=0.644; with Group B tol0=0.555 (worse), tol1 flat at 0.790, tol2 improves to 0.840. Net negative at the primary paragraph-exact granularity. predict()'s new position_scores parameter (added this session) stays unused by default -- kept only so scripts/s6_group_b_position_scores_eval.py can test through the real codepath without duplicating it.
- **Reason:** Same root cause as the Group-C finding: entity_surface_matches records what entities are PRESENT in a resolution's text, not where a resolution OPENS -- feeding it through the same phrase_weight/dispersion bonus-vs-even-split tradeoff segment_gap uses for opening-phrase hits pulls cuts toward entity-dense paragraphs irrespective of whether they start a resolution. Two independent evidence sources (Group B and Group C) have now failed the same way through the same scoring mechanism, which is stronger evidence the mechanism itself -- not the evidence choice -- is the limiting factor. Densifying weak_separation via position_scores tuning is likely exhausted without a smarter in-gap signal (e.g. phrase-hit-style start-specific evidence only, or per-entity role weighting, both already named as untried in the 2026-09-18 split-POC session notes).

## 2026-09-23: Correction: Group-B and Group-C position_scores regressions are not the same magnitude or mechanism

- **Context:** Follow-up to the 2026-09-23 'Group-B position_scores wiring measured negative' decision, which claimed Group B and Group C 'fail the same way through the same scoring mechanism' -- the user pushed back on that as implausible and asked for concrete examples. Re-ran the same day-by-day predicted-position comparison (baseline vs. with-evidence) for BOTH groups, restricted to the 19-21 scoreable gold days, through predict()'s real codepath.
- **Decision:** The claim was an overreach; retracted. Group B: 11/19 scoreable days changed prediction, ALL 11 strictly worse (net -21 true-positive cuts across those days, e.g. 16->12 on 1630-03-20). Group C: 8/21 days changed, 6 unchanged-TP / 2 worse / 0 better (net -2 true-positive cuts total) -- an order of magnitude milder. Traced Group B's larger damage to a concrete, identified cause: entity_surface_matches_1626_1630 is keyed by flat RESOLUTION id, not paragraph id, so every axis paragraph belonging to a multi-paragraph flat resolution gets an IDENTICAL bonus (verified directly on 1626-01-08: resolution-12 spans 4 axis paragraphs, resolution-2 spans 3, all sub-paragraphs share one score). Combined with near-saturated match rates (12/13 resolutions matched at 85-100 confidence on that day), the bonus is a near-flat high plateau across almost the whole axis -- it nudges an already-good even-split default toward arbitrary high-score paragraphs uncorrelated with true boundaries. Group C's phrase-hit evidence, despite similarly high paragraph coverage (6-17 of 8-20 paragraphs per day), is computed AT paragraph granularity to begin with, which is the more likely reason its damage stays small.
- **Reason:** Correct diagnosis matters for what to try next: this is evidence that position_scores evidence CAN be near-neutral (Group C) or even beneficial if it is genuinely paragraph-localized, not that in-gap content evidence is a dead end. The dominant fixable variable is Group B's flat_id-level granularity mismatch, not the position_scores mechanism itself. Do not cite the retracted 'mechanism exhausted' framing going forward; see this entry instead.

## 2026-09-23: entity_surface_matches' one-sided 'new' recoveries are almost entirely ungrounded (1.6%) -- confirms two-sided design is required, not optional

- **Context:** Follow-up to the 45.2% one-sided headroom finding (2026-09-23) and the user's request to run the decisive two-sided check before deciding whether to extend align_session's lookup. Sampled 40 random dates (seed=42) across the full 1626-1630 corpus, resolved each date's enriched resolutions' own known entities via build_alignment_new.py's resolve_enriched_entities (already-proven code, unmodified), and checked how many of the 243 sampled 'new' entity_surface_matches names match ANY specific enriched resolution that day (not just float unattached).
- **Decision:** Only 4/243 (1.6%) are grounded to a specific enriched resolution's own entity list; 98.4% are floating. Sanity-checked directly (1626-01-19): genuinely-grounded enriched entities are small, specific place names (Oudenbosch, Kempenland, Zevenbergen, Hooge Zwaluwe); the floating flagged names are large, common geographic/political terms (Holland, Zeeland, Engeland, Amsterdam, Groningen, Algiers) that plausibly appear as incidental context in most any resolution's narration, not as that resolution's own subject. This is not a bug in the check (resolve_enriched_entities verified working correctly on real data) -- it is a real property of entity_surface_matches' recoveries.
- **Reason:** Retroactively explains why feeding Group B's one-sided presence signal into segment_day's position_scores regressed so badly earlier this session: a signal that is 98.4% ungrounded fires as near-uniform noise, exactly the near-saturated-high-plateau pattern already diagnosed on 1626-01-08. It also decisively validates the 'one unequivocal entity-overlap layer' design discussed with the user: any consolidated table MUST require genuine two-sided grounding (a specific candidate enriched resolution's own resolved entities present in the flat text), not one-sided presence-in-text, or 98%+ of a naive fuzzy-recovery pass would need to be treated as anchor-quality evidence when it is not. Do not treat entity_surface_matches (or s4_fuzzy_surface_form_scan) as anchor-ready without this two-sided filter applied first.

## 2026-09-23: Day-level grounding of Group-B partially recovers but does not close the regression

- **Context:** docs/DECISIONS.md 2026-09-23 found entity_surface_matches' one-sided 'new' recoveries are 98.4% ungrounded (1.6%/243 match a specific enriched resolution's own known entities), explaining Group-B position_scores' measured regression (tol0 0.644->0.555). Built scripts/s6_group_b_grounded_position_scores_eval.py to test the implied fix: restrict Group-B's bonus to matches whose canonical_name is grounded (present in the UNION of that calendar date's own enriched resolutions' resolved entity sets via resolve_enriched_entities), same 19/21 gold-day harness as the ungrounded eval.
- **Decision:** Day-level grounding is a real but partial fix, not sufficient to wire in: tol0 F1 0.555 (ungrounded) -> 0.588 (grounded), still below the 0.644 no-evidence baseline; tol1 0.790->0.784 (slightly worse); tol2 0.840->0.824 (still better than baseline's 0.807 but worse than ungrounded). Do not wire grounded Group-B into the live predictor on this result. 101/181 (56%) of flagged paragraphs survive day-level grounding, far above the 1.6% grounded share the corpus-wide 40-day two-sided sample found -- day-level grounding is coarser than per-resolution grounding (it only checks whether ANY enriched resolution that date claims the name, not the specific paragraph's true owner), so it still lets through same-day cross-resolution false positives.
- **Reason:** Confirms groundedness was a real contributing cause (partial recovery in the predicted direction) but not the whole story -- the day-level check is too permissive on multi-resolution days where several enriched resolutions share common entity names (e.g. 'Holland', 'Staten Generaal'-adjacent terms). A tighter per-paragraph/per-candidate-assignment grounding check would need to know the placement the predictor is trying to produce, which is circular for a pre-computed position_scores bonus; the next viable direction (if this lever is pursued further) is per-entity specificity weighting (rare/short place names like the 1626-01-19 spot-check's Oudenbosch/Kempenland vs common ones like Holland/Amsterdam), not day-level presence.

## 2026-09-23: Span-gap pairwise bridging: even the most generous defensible relaxation falls short of the Global-spans stretch criterion

- **Context:** svz.py review's own pick for this session: map why solid days (693/1594) fail to form >=30-day spans, since severe_collapse_implicated flipped False and its reopening rationale no longer held. Generalized metrics_span_gap_map.py's single-class bridge counterfactual to pairwise combinations of the three populated nonsolid_class values (missing_htr 329, nihil_actum 127, weak_separation 445).
- **Decision:** Single-class bridges: nihil_actum alone -> longest 12->31d (1 span/31d); missing_htr alone -> no change (12d); weak_separation alone -> 20d. Pairwise: missing_htr+nihil_actum (the two classes with no genuine algorithmic-failure evidence -- no business done, or no HTR text to judge) -> still only 31d/1 span, short of the >=6 spans/>=180 days stretch criterion. Only missing_htr+weak_separation jointly clears it (51d longest, 9 spans, 366 days, criterion_met=True) -- but that requires bridging weak_separation, a REAL measured placement-quality gap, not an evidence gap. Conclusion: even treating every day with no basis to judge failure as non-breaking isn't enough; weak_separation (placement quality) must also improve for the stretch criterion to be reachable. Do not pursue cheap bridging as a substitute for placement-quality work.
- **Reason:** Confirms, from an independent angle (span geography, not the position_scores gold-day harness), the same conclusion the exhausted position_scores experiments (Group B/C, 3 variants) already pointed to: placement quality on weak_separation days is the binding constraint on Global spans, not data/axis coverage. The two remaining candidate levers (per-paragraph .find()-relocated Group-B evidence; the full two-sided entity-overlap consolidation table) are both already flagged as multi-session investments needing an explicit go/no-go, not a default next action.

## 2026-09-23: resolved_session_id has no cross-date uniqueness constraint -- likely driver of weak_separation clustering, found via user-proposed windowed K_e-vs-K_f drift check

- **Context:** User asked (1) where's the biggest expected improvement, and (2) whether a windowed K_e-vs-HTR-count comparison (to detect session/date shift) is already investigated, proposing it be scoped to specific windows rather than run corpus-wide. Built scripts/metrics_ke_kf_window_drift_diagnostic.py (10/10 tests), scoped to metrics_span_gap_map's 13 longest weak_separation-dominated breaking gaps: per-day residual (k_e - paragraph_count), lag-1 autocorrelation, and sign-flipping adjacent-pair share.
- **Decision:** 12/13 flagged windows show a shift_candidate signature (alternating over-/under-attribution between adjacent days), only 1/13 a uniform capacity shortfall. Spot-checked the #1-ranked window (1627-03-25..1627-04-10, 15 nonsolid days) directly against resolution_concordance_1626_1630: 1627-03-27 and 1627-03-29 both carry resolved_session_id=session-3186-num-55 (identical paragraph_count=53); 1627-04-01 and 1627-04-03 both carry session-3186-num-57 (identical paragraph_count=77) -- two different enriched calendar dates independently resolved to the SAME underlying HTR session. Traced to source: scripts/s4_day_status_resolution.py's resolve_row() picks each ledger row's (i.e. each enriched date's) top_candidate_session_id from s4_candidate_scoring_predictions independently, per-date, with no constraint that a session can be claimed by only one date. Quantified corpus-wide: 68/1053 resolved_auto sessions (6.5%) are claimed by 2+ enriched dates, 153 dates (~10% of resolved_auto dates) involved. Recommend this as the next concrete unit of work over further position_scores tuning: decide a uniqueness policy (best-score-wins per session, with the loser falling back to its next candidate or to uncertain) and re-run the day-status/concordance chain to measure the effect on weak_separation and Global spans.
- **Reason:** This is a structural mapping gap with concrete before/after evidence (not a data-quality ceiling like missing_htr, and not another position_scores evidence variant -- three of those already failed through the same in-day-placement mechanism). It directly explains why the worst weak_separation stretches cluster rather than scattering randomly, and is independently corroborated by three prior signals this project already had: the axis_for_date richer_session_ignored bug (9 cases, fixed 2026-09-23), s6b_session_fingerprint_match's 62% drifted-offset rate, and the unreviewed 301-row session-date mapping queue. Answers the user's 'where's the biggest expected improvement' question with a specific, sized, traced mechanism rather than a general prioritization argument.

## 2026-09-23: Corpus-wide collision-vs-weak_separation crosstab confirms and sizes the mechanism: 27.6% of weak_separation days are collision-involved

- **Context:** Follow-up to the 2026-09-23 'resolved_session_id has no cross-date uniqueness constraint' decision, which spot-checked one window and sampled 13 windows via a heuristic (autocorrelation/sign-flip) shift-signature score. User asked to check specifically against the weak_separation days blocking spans, not just a window sample -- extended scripts/metrics_ke_kf_window_drift_diagnostic.py with session_collision_dates() and collision_weak_separation_crosstab() (2 new tests, 12/12 passing) to join ALL resolved_session_id collisions against the full Tier O day series (1,138 resolved_auto days), not just the 13 flagged windows.
- **Decision:** Collision dates (n=153) are weak_separation 80.4% of the time vs 32.7% for non-collision dates -- a 2.5x effect size, not a coincidence of the window sample. Population-level: 123/445 (27.6%) of ALL weak_separation days corpus-wide carry a session-collision. Registered output now includes a corpus_collision_crosstab record alongside the per-window signatures.
- **Reason:** Answers the user's 'check specifically against the weak_separation days blocking spans' request rigorously rather than by window-sample extrapolation. Over a quarter of the exact population blocking Global spans is explained by one traced, fixable mapping bug in scripts/s4_day_status_resolution.py's resolve_row() (no cross-date uniqueness constraint on top_candidate_session_id) -- strengthens the case for prioritizing a uniqueness-policy fix there over further position_scores tuning, which only ever addressed in-day placement and could never reach this 27.6%.

## 2026-09-23: Uniqueness policy wired into resolve_row(); re-measured -- real but partial effect, paragraph-level separation not yet propagated

- **Context:** Follow-up to the two decisions above. Before this fix, s4_day_status_resolution.py's resolve_row() read each candidate-scoring row's top_candidate_session_id independently with no cross-date constraint. Checked first whether the collision was specific to candidate_scoring or also present in ledger_direct (T/E) rows: querying s4_day_status_resolution directly found BOTH sources collide (candidate_scoring 123/129 resolved_auto rows collision-involved; ledger_direct 87/1,109) -- but the traced mechanism and both prior decisions scoped the fix to candidate_scoring's top_candidate_session_id specifically, so ledger_direct collisions are left untouched and out of scope for this pass (T/E rows aren't ranked picks -- there's no "next candidate" to fall back to, and a session legitimately spanning ledger rows for adjacent dates via overlapping inventory metadata can't be ruled out without separate investigation).
- **Decision:** Added `deduplicate_session_claims()` (scripts/s4_day_status_resolution.py, 6 new tests, 16/16 passing) as a preprocessing pass over `s4_candidate_scoring_predictions` before `resolve_row()` runs: global greedy assignment over every (row, ranked_candidate) pair sorted by descending combined_score -- highest score claims its row and session first; any later pair naming an already-claimed row or an already-claimed session is skipped, which is exactly "loser falls back to its own next-ranked confident candidate" since that pair appears later in the same sorted list; a row that exhausts every confident candidate becomes `low_confidence=True` (-> `uncertain` in `resolve_row`, unchanged). No rescoring needed -- `ranked_candidates` was already persisted per row by `s4_candidate_scoring_batch.py`. `resolve_row()` itself is unchanged.

  Re-ran the chain (`s4_day_status_resolution` -> `s4_resolution_concordance` -> `metrics_nihil_actum_invariant` -> `metrics_separation_span_table` -> `metrics_span_gap_map` -> `metrics_ke_kf_window_drift_diagnostic`), then, since `axis_for_date` (`s4_corpus_paragraph_predictions.py`) itself falls back to `resolution_concordance_1626_1630`'s `resolved_session_id`, re-ran `s4_corpus_paragraph_predictions` too (cheap, ~2.5s, per its 2026-09-21 precedent) and repeated `s4_resolution_concordance` + the Tier O chain a second time so paragraph attribution reflects the corrected session mapping, not the stale one. All told <15s of compute.

  `resolved_auto` day-status rows 1,238 -> 1,202 (36 rows, mostly collision losers with no confident fallback, moved to `uncertain`). Collision dates corpus-wide 153 -> 132 (-13.7%); `weak_separation` days 445 -> 430 (-15); `weak_separation` collision share 27.6% -> 23.9% (123/445 -> 102/426) -- the collision mechanism itself is fixed as designed, and the remaining 132 collision dates are consistent with the untouched 87 ledger_direct collisions (out of scope, see above) plus residual candidate_scoring ties on rows that had zero fallback candidates. 75 days' resolved axis changed content after the predictor refresh.

  **But the headline metrics did not move**: after the full two-pass propagation, Global-primary separation is 8,015/11,644 = 68.8% of ceiling -- identical to the already-recorded 2026-09-23 baseline, not the 8,020 seen after only the first (predictor-stale) pass. Spans@30 unchanged (4 spans/177 days at gap=2, longest 60d). `weak_separation` day count in `metrics_span_gap_map` also held at 430 across both passes, i.e. the predictor refresh changed *which* axis 75 days use but not enough of them flipped from weak to solid (or vice versa) to move the count.
- **Reason:** The collision-count and weak_separation-collision-share drops are genuine, measured, and mechanism-consistent -- the fix does what it was designed to do (68/1,053 collided sessions is no longer possible for the candidate_scoring path). But collision-freeness at the day-status level is necessary, not sufficient, for a date's paragraph-level placement to actually improve: a date that stops colliding still inherits whatever `segment_gap`'s in-gap DP produces on its now-uncontested session, and that placement-quality layer is exactly what three prior `position_scores` experiments (Group B/C, this session's cycle) already found near-exhausted. This is a second, independent confirmation of the same standing conclusion (docs/DECISIONS.md 2026-09-23 "Span-gap pairwise bridging"): **placement quality on `weak_separation` days, not which session a date maps to, is now the binding constraint** -- fixing the mapping bug was necessary to rule it out as a confound, but it was not itself the lever that moves Global spans. Do not expect further mapping-uniqueness work (e.g. extending this policy to ledger_direct T/E collisions) to move the headline numbers either, absent new evidence it targets placement quality specifically.

Asked the user whether the >=30-day contiguous-span stretch criterion actually matters for the downstream use case before deciding whether to invest in the multi-session S6 segmentation DP. User confirmed: the downstream consumer only needs per-resolution placement, not 30-day contiguous coverage.

(2) User greenlit the S6 segmentation DP (scripts/s6c_gap_segmentation.py's segment_gap/segment_day, s6-anchor-chain-alignment track) as the one credible untried lever to close the 718-resolution stretch gap, since position_scores evidence tuning (Group B/C, 3 variants) and the session-uniqueness fix are both now measured exhausted/flat on this metric.

Records the go decision on the S6 DP explicitly, per docs/ITERATION_POLICY.md's rule that a multi-session investment needs an explicit go/no-go from whoever reads the report, not a default pickup -- that call is now made and traceable.

## 2026-09-23: Per-paragraph .find()-relocated Group-B evidence measured negative -- granularity-mismatch hypothesis does not recover the regression

- **Context:** User picked lever (a) of the three untried S6 placement-quality candidates (docs/DECISIONS.md 2026-09-23 greenlight): per-paragraph .find()-relocated Group-B evidence, which docs/DECISIONS.md's 'Correction' entry diagnosed as the dominant fixable variable behind Group-B's -21 TP regression (entity_surface_matches_1626_1630 is keyed by flat resolution id, so every sub-paragraph of a multi-paragraph resolution shares one identical bonus).
- **Decision:** Built scripts/s6_group_b_relocated_position_scores_eval.py (group_b_relocated_position_scores: exact substring, fuzz.partial_ratio fallback at build_entity_surface_matches.py's own validated threshold 85, applied per axis paragraph's own text instead of per whole flat-resolution text), 4 new tests (4/4 passing), registered s6_group_b_relocated_position_scores_eval in the manifest. Same 19/21 gold-day harness through predict()'s real codepath. Result: tol0 F1 0.543 (worse than blanket Group-B's 0.555, further from baseline 0.644); tol1 0.773 (worse than blanket 0.790); tol2 0.840 (tied with blanket). A follow-up ad hoc exact-only variant (fuzzy fallback removed entirely) scored 0.560 -- still net negative and barely above the fuzzy-relocated result. Do not wire either variant into the live predictor.
- **Reason:** Spot-checked 1626-01-08 (the same day docs/DECISIONS.md's Correction entry used): relocation correctly dropped the bonus on 2/18 paragraphs where the name was genuinely paragraph-level absent, but left it on almost all others, because most flagged names recur (genuinely or via permissive short-string fuzz.partial_ratio) across nearly every paragraph of their source resolution, not just the one that originally justified the flat-level match. The exact-only control isolates fuzzy-fallback noise as the cause and rules it out: even zero-false-positive-risk exact relocation stays net negative. This closes lever (a) as tested -- the granularity-mismatch diagnosis was correct as far as it went (Group B's damage genuinely traces to flat-id-level bonus sharing) but fixing it is not sufficient to recover the baseline, meaning the position_scores bonus-vs-even-split mechanism itself remains the dominant limiting factor, consistent with the standing 2026-09-23 conclusion. Remaining untried levers from the same greenlit list: (b) per-entity role/type weighting in segment_gap's cost function, (c) the full two-sided entity-overlap consolidation table -- next session should pick one via explicit go/no-go, per docs/ITERATION_POLICY.md.

## 2026-09-23: Gap-candidate-grounded Group-B tested (lever c, cheap form) -- ties day-level grounding exactly, closing position_scores tuning

- **Context:** User go/no-go (2026-09-23) picked lever (c), the full two-sided entity-overlap consolidation table, as the next untried S6c placement-quality lever after (a) per-paragraph .find()-relocation was closed the same day (docs/DECISIONS.md). Building the full 4-phase consolidation table (unify build_windowed_overlap.py/build_per_overlap.py/entity_surface_matches/s4_fuzzy_surface_form_scan into one table read by both align_session/lookup and build_alignment_new.py::resolve_shared_entities) is a genuine multi-session migration, so ran its cheap gold-day-harness precursor first per this cycle's own methodology: does grounding Group-B's bonus to a GAP's specific candidate resolution(s) -- the tightest two-sided grounding possible without the full table -- beat the already-tested day-level grounding (0.555->0.588)? Non-circular: segment_day's count-constrained DP already knows which enriched-resolution indices occupy a gap before choosing where, from the gap's count alone, so scripts/s6_group_b_candidate_grounded_position_scores_eval.py (5 new tests) recomputes that same anchor backbone read-only and scopes resolve_enriched_entities per gap instead of per day.
- **Decision:** Do not build the full two-sided consolidation table on this evidence. Gap-candidate grounding ties day-level grounding EXACTLY at tol0 (0.588 vs 0.588; candidate_id_match_rate=1.0, so this isn't a data-matching artifact) and is marginally worse at tol1 (0.779 vs 0.784), marginally better at tol2 (0.829 vs 0.824) -- a wash, not an improvement, both still well below the 0.644 no-evidence baseline. Closes lever (c) as tested. Combined with lever (a)'s four exhausted variants (blanket/day-grounded/relocated/exact-relocated, all also negative), position_scores tuning is now exhausted across five tested variants total. Only lever (b) (per-entity role/type weighting in segment_gap's cost function) remains untried from the 2026-09-23 greenlit list; next session should either try (b) or accept the current S6c result (68.8% of ceiling, Global-primary MET) as this track's outcome, per docs/ITERATION_POLICY.md.
- **Reason:** The result is informative precisely because it's a null result at maximum achievable precision: if the tightest possible grounding (per-gap-candidate, matched 87/87=100%) can't beat the coarser day-level version, the full consolidation table -- which would deliver exactly this same per-candidate grounding property via more machinery, at multi-session cost -- has no remaining headroom to find. The binding constraint is confirmed to be the position_scores bonus-vs-even-split mechanism itself (entity presence signals content, not resolution-opening position), not evidence granularity at any level tested so far.

## 2026-09-25: Step 1 gate PASSED: session order is reliable where the session label is not

- **Context:** plans/archive/COLLISION_AVOIDANCE_TRACK.md Step 1 is the gate for the whole inventory-stream reframing: landmarks must re-impose locality or trading k_e-per-day for k_e-per-inventory is a net loss. Measured by scripts/landmark_density_eval.py (dataset landmark_density_eval, 14/14 tests), thresholds fixed before the run; the day-partition arm reproduces the cited 11,644 ceiling and 535 no-HTR dates exactly and the one-segment arm reproduces finding C's 14,258.
- **Decision:** PASS, and pursue the reframing via content-verified session ORDER rather than via better opening detection. Availability is not the constraint (date region in 100% of 1,690 raw sessions, first region 99.8%, attendance 71.7%); localizability on the paragraph_axis stream reaches 86.0% of 1,316 flat sessions across three complementary channels. The decisive result is reliability: zero non-monotone steps among content-verified landmarks in all five annual inventories, while 80-99% of session labels carry a non-zero raw-vs-flat offset in only 5-11 piecewise runs. Next step is Step 4 (anchor-correction loop), not Step 3.
- **Reason:** The order-preserving verified chain is the scaffold Step 4 said it needed and it already exists on disk, whereas Step 3 still owes a gap/scoring design. Two limits are recorded so the PASS is not over-read: locality genuinely loosens (2.13 dates per segment, median segment k_e 1.33x the day median), and most of the raw +1,329 ceiling gain is the 535 no-HTR dates being absorbed into neighbours -- only +342 survives when they are barred, which is below the 718 Global-stretch gap. So the reframing is usable but does not by itself close Global-stretch at honest locality, and PLAN.md's targets stay unrestated per Step 5.

## 2026-09-25: Step 3 unblocked by a parameter-free formulation; my Step 4-first ranking was readiness, not value

- **Context:** After Step 1 passed I recommended Step 4 ahead of Step 3 because Step 3 'owes a gap/scoring design'. The user pushed back that this is a problem not to sidestep, then proposed edit distance for the entity-sequence alignment. Code check: align_session (build_alignment_new.py:900) aligns the resolution sequence and entities enter only as a per-cell bag (:931); anchor_only_diagonal sends every zero-overlap cell to NO_ANCHOR_DIAG_SCORE (:938-941), which with containment 0.295 is shut most of the time. The overlap tables keep a 4-way match_kind category and no similarity column, and nw_matches_gt_rate is 0.957 correct vs 0.962 false-positive.
- **Decision:** Take Step 3 next, not Step 4. Move the alignment's unit from resolution to entity mention inside Step 1's verified-landmark segments and test it with unit-cost edit distance -- parameter-free, so the 'settle the scoring design first' blocker dissolves rather than being deferred. Keep the traceback; fix gap asymmetry before running; accept transposition over-penalisation as a conservative bias. Graded surface similarity as substitution cost only if that run beats the 0.957/0.962 null. Gate: D1c re-run stratified by IDF band.
- **Reason:** The readiness argument was not a value argument. On value the ranking inverts: Step 3 attacks finding A's dominant loss channel (collisions 4:1 over extent) and finding B's 1,478 directly, whereas Step 1's order-preservation result narrows Step 4's target -- order is already intact among the 71.4% verified, leaving mostly the unverified 28.6%. Hypothesis 5 is also currently untestable rather than untested, which makes the entity-unit change a prerequisite for judging the whole idea. track-b-name-matching (soundex/Levenshtein) was closed 2026-09-23 unmeasured on a prior, so this is not a repeat of a negative result.

## 2026-09-25: External survey read: four design options named, first experiment unchanged

- **Context:** User supplied Prousalis et al., 'A Survey on Sequence Alignment Algorithms and State-of-the-Art Aligners' (ACM CSUR 2025), read in full, and asked whether it changes views or contributes metrics. Its MSA half (star/progressive/iterative/genetic/swarm/HMM/DAC/ML) and its modern-aspects section (quantum, optics) do not apply -- this problem is pairwise. Its benchmarking indicators (reads-aligned %, mapping efficiency, BAliScore, TC-score, sum-of-pairs) do not transfer: they presuppose a reference genome or a curated reference alignment, and the survey itself warns its benchmarks are not comparable across studies.
- **Decision:** Direction unchanged; no metrics adopted from it. Recorded four pairwise-section items in Step 3 of plans/archive/COLLISION_AVOIDANCE_TRACK.md as a post-first-experiment menu, citing primaries (LAST/Kielbasa 2011 adaptive seeds for the dense alphabet; minimap2/Li 2018 concave gap cost for the gap asymmetry; semi-global free end gaps for cross-day spillover; MUMmer/Delcher 1999 MUM+LIS for 4562's non-monotone landmarks) with an explicit guard that the first experiment stays unit-cost and parameter-free. Separately logged a MAPQ-style placement margin under docs/METRICS.md 'Proposed, not yet computed'.
- **Reason:** The survey is a pointer to primary references, not evidence about this corpus, so it changes design options rather than findings -- recorded as such. The guard exists because appending a menu of sophisticated alternatives beside a deliberately parameter-free experiment is exactly how the 'design a scoring scheme before testing the hypothesis' blocker would get rebuilt. Two independent corroborations noted without action: minimap2's seed-chain-extend is the reference implementation of what STEP_S6 designed independently, and the survey's 'banded DP is the bottleneck for long sequences' matches what the S6 Step-1 oracle diagnostic and the S6b anchor-supply harvest each measured here.

## 2026-09-25: Post-mortem code check closed; three carried-forward candidates recorded

- **Context:** plans/POST_MORTEM_CODE_CHECK.md completed all 5 items 2026-09-25 but never wrote the DECISIONS.md entry its own Deliverable section requires, leaving three confirmed findings living only in that document. Found during a limbo sweep of plans/ and state.json.
- **Decision:** Close the post-mortem. Carry forward three candidates, none a correctness bug, none blocking: (1) a data_io.check --freshness extension comparing a dataset's recorded parent_sources/timestamp against its parents' current provenance -- the resolution_concordance staleness that gated the entire Tier O baseline for a week would be equally invisible today; (2) the dead loc_names lookup for places in build_alignment_new.py:342-377, 0% hit rate over 21,184 place references, correct only by accident of the identity fallback; (3) s6b_anchor_harvest.py's output is orphaned -- 589 lines writing a dataset nothing loads -- worth a note in APPROACH_OVERVIEW's orphaned-work section rather than deletion.
- **Reason:** Findings held in a single plan document evaporate when that document closes, which is the same failure mode the post-mortem itself was investigating (the fuzzy-surface-form-scan track's answer existed under a different task id and was never written back). Candidate (1) is the only one with reuse value beyond this repo and is the one worth scoping as real work; (2) and (3) are cleanups that should not be done inline under a read-only audit.

## 2026-09-25: Step 5c.1: the separated set is accurate enough to freeze as a scaffold, except on no-HTR days

- **Context:** Step 5 of plans/archive/COLLISION_AVOIDANCE_TRACK.md proposed treating the 8,015 separated resolutions (68.8% of the 11,644 ceiling) as a frozen scaffold and attacking the gaps around it. Condition 5c.1 blocked that until accuracy was verified, because 'separated' is a coverage criterion (unique start paragraph, extent <=3) that never tests correctness, and Step 2 had shown a trivial uniform spread beating gold's own annotations on it.
- **Decision:** Adopt the scaffold framing, excluding review-code-M (no same-day HTR) days. Measured on 35 boundary-gold days via scripts/separated_accuracy_eval.py: separated placements hit the exact gold start paragraph 62.8% of the time vs 30.6% for non-separated, and 86.9% land within one paragraph vs 62.0%. Production beats the uniform null 2.4-2.7x on correctness (0.628 vs 0.267 separated; 0.503 vs 0.189 overall), answering the doubt Step 2 raised. On gold's segmentable days accuracy is 63.9% exact / 91.1% within one paragraph, median displacement 0. On missing-HTR days it collapses to 27.1% exact with mean displacement 5.87 paragraphs, because axis_for_date falls back to a resolved neighbouring session and places resolutions on borrowed paragraphs.
- **Reason:** Selecting on 'separated' genuinely selects better placements, which is what a scaffold needs; that is a different job from ranking placement rules, which 'separated' still cannot do (uniform 115 vs gold 103 vs production 104 on the 15 fully-located gold days, recomputed under the canonical gold convention). Freezing M-day placements as anchors would be the exact failure mode condition 1 exists to prevent, since a wrong anchor corrupts the gap on each side of it. Sample caveat: the gold sample over-weights hard days (25 of 35 scored days are diff_3plus), so these figures are pessimistic rather than flattering.

## 2026-09-25: Resolution-level alignment: insertions are not a modelled event; encode as a structural constraint, not a penalty

- **Context:** User observation 2026-09-25 while Step 3 (entity-stream edit alignment) was being designed: at the resolution level, deletion is the norm, transposition is rare, and no genuine insertion has ever been observed -- 'in this respect it differs from aDNA procedures', where reads do carry real insertions. The track file had left gap asymmetry as an open 'fix before running' item with no direction attached.
- **Decision:** Forbid the enriched-side gap move outright rather than pricing it, and make the HTR-side 'stay on the same enriched resolution' move zero or near-zero cost -- i.e. a monotone many-to-many (stepwise) alignment, not Levenshtein with reweighted gaps. Concave gap cost is promoted from optional to the natural fit in the prior-art menu. Direction is level-specific: at the entity-mention level HTR-side skips stay cheap (the summary drops detail the HTR keeps); at the resolution level the asymmetry inverts.
- **Reason:** Checked against gold rather than accepted on assertion: boundary_gold_sample's instructions are 'place K_e - 1 cut points over the ordered stream', and its slot vocabulary (kind in cut/start/end_of_last_resolution, plus has_trailing_spillover and starts_mid_resolution) has NO category for an HTR span belonging to no enriched resolution. The ground truth's own design is a surjection of the stream onto exactly K_e contiguous spans, so the no-insertion prior is gold's construction, not merely consistent with it. The trap a bare penalty falls into: K_f > K_e on 10 of the 50 gold days (1626-11-16 is 47 flat records against 14 resolutions), so under a strict one-to-one NW those surplus records MUST take enriched-side gaps -- raising the penalty does not suppress them, it buys them off by forcing wrong substitutions. The surplus is HTR over-segmentation, the mirror of the measured 81.1% under-segmentation; neither is an insertion.

## 2026-09-25: Nihil-actum is a skip constraint on the enriched stream, not a two-sided alignment anchor

- **Context:** User proposed (2026-09-25) using the nihil-actum signal as anchors in Step 6's session-level sequence alignment. Checked both sides against artifacts already on disk before wiring anything in.
- **Decision:** Do not wire nihil-actum in as an anchor class. Use the 127 enriched nihil sittings as a hard a-priori skip constraint (they must consume zero HTR sessions), and harvest the HTR-side nihil/vacat/niet-gedaen marker dates separately as their own landmark inventory.
- **Reason:** The two sides are not the same class. Enriched nihil is a calendar artifact, not a content event: 112 of the 127 dates (88%) are Sundays, against an otherwise even 227-228 per weekday across the enriched date series, and only 2 of the 127 appear in paragraph_axis_1626_1630 at all -- a day with no sitting produced no text, so there is nothing to anchor to. The HTR side carries its own markers on 138 dates (nihil actum 39, vacat 103, niet gedaen 16 paragraphs) with exactly zero date overlap with the enriched set. As a skip constraint the enriched set is valuable precisely because 46.6% of sessions are already mis-keyed by more than one position: 127 guaranteed no-consume positions stop the aligner shifting everything downstream by one. Caveat: the HTR-side 138 comes from crude case-folded substring matching with no orthographic variant sweep, so it is an upper bound; the enriched-side 127 is exact.

## 2026-09-25: Correction: the HTR-side nihil marker set is 32 dates, not 138; the collapse is mostly implicit

- **Context:** A 2026-09-25 entry ('Nihil-actum is a skip constraint...') reported 138 HTR-side marker dates from case-folded substring matching over paragraph_axis_1626_1630, and proposed harvesting them as an independent landmark inventory. User challenged both the marker set and the landmark reading.
- **Decision:** Retract the 138 figure and withdraw the landmark-inventory recommendation. The usable HTR marker set is 32 dates. Keep the enriched-side skip constraint, which the correction strengthens.
- **Reason:** 119 of the 138 were false positives, found by reading the matched text: all 103 'vacat' hits are 'vacatien', per-diem FEES from Latin vacatio in travel-expense declarations, and all 16 'niet gedaen' hits are ordinary prose. Of 46 'nihil' paragraphs only 32 are standalone Nihil Actum markers. The collapse of a nihil sitting into a neighbouring session is mostly IMPLICIT (user): 32 of 127 leave any trace, 95 leave none. That makes the skip constraint essential rather than optional -- with no marker on 75% of cases, an aligner has no local evidence from the HTR side that a sitting is missing and must be told a priori from the enriched side. The 32 that do leave a trace sit at session ends (28 of 32 within two paragraphs of the last) and concentrate one day before the nearest enriched nihil date (17 of 32; enriched 88% Sunday, markers 59% Monday), but direction cannot be settled because the flat session date label is itself the drifted quantity. 32 events is too thin to key an alignment on. Method note: the 138 was reported before the matched text was read.

## 2026-09-25: Step 6's gate: the enriched<->HTR session mapping is already complete wherever there is HTR to map to

- **Context:** plans/archive/COLLISION_AVOIDANCE_TRACK.md Step 6 was ranked ahead of Step 3 on the argument that the enriched->HTR session correspondence is established by date label plus a +/-1-day window, and that 5,619 resolutions (29.4% of 19,120) sit on dates whose session mapping is 'absent or already known to be wrong'. Its own first deliverable was a gate diagnostic measuring the enriched<->HTR session offset across the streams, rather than arguing by analogy from s6b_session_fingerprint_match's flat<->raw offsets (both sides HTR).
- **Decision:** Gate does NOT pass; verdict INCONCLUSIVE against thresholds fixed before the run (determined_share 0.9% vs >=25% required; beyond-window share 100% vs >=50% required). Do not build the session aligner on this evidence. scripts/session_offset_eval.py (+ 13 tests) -> dataset session_offset_eval. The step's factual premise does not hold for the main corpus: in inventories 3185-3189 EVERY enriched sitting already resolves to an HTR session (1,089/1,089) and the concordance has ZERO missing_htr rows there. All 344 unresolved sittings / 4,482 resolutions are in 4562 (2,095) and 4861 (2,387) -- the two non-annual 'secret resolution' series PLAN.md's Local criterion already excludes, where 4861 has no axis sessions at all and 4562 has 102 sessions for 187 sittings. Only 3 sittings (40 resolutions, all 4562) sit in determined brackets. The 1,137 cross_day_shift resolutions on 74 dates are NOT unmapped -- every one carries a resolved session. Two by-products: the no-insertion claim, verified at session level as the design notes required, is nearly but not exactly true (51/1,316 sessions have no enriched sitting; 12 outside 4562); and the enriched edition ends 1630-05-14 while the axis runs to 1630-12-31, so 180 trailing 3189 sessions lie outside the enriched span.
- **Reason:** Step 6's mechanism is confirmed where it applies -- all 3 order-forced pairings are 20/22/36 days from their date label, correspondences the +/-1-day window provably could not reach -- but it applies to 40 of 19,120 resolutions against a Global-stretch gap of 718. Building a content-keyed session aligner to serve 4861 (no HTR) and 4562 (anchor-starved, already excluded from pooling by finding C and carrying Step 1's only 15 non-monotone landmarks) is the expensive path to the least reachable mass in the corpus. NOT tested here, and the honest caveat on this verdict: that a sitting is pinned does not mean it is pinned CORRECTLY, so this closes 'the mapping is absent' as a motivation without settling 'the mapping is wrong'.

## 2026-09-25: The enriched edition ends 1630-05-14; the alignment does not extend beyond it

- **Context:** Confirmed by the user 2026-09-25 after the Step 6 gate diagnostic surfaced that inventory 3189 carries 289 axis sessions against only 108 enriched sittings. Measured: the paragraph_axis_1626_1630 runs to 1630-12-31 and holds 2,284 paragraphs (10.6% of 21,519) over 184 sessions dated after the enriched edition's last sitting -- 180 in 3189, 4 in 4562.
- **Decision:** Treat 1630-05-14 as a hard scope boundary on the HTR side, not just a descriptive fact. scripts/session_offset_eval.py drops post-edition sessions from the stream before bracketing (cut-off derived from the data, not hardcoded) and asserts that no enriched sitting resolves into them; that count is 0 today, so the bar costs nothing now and exists to catch a widened axis_for_date neighbour fallback later. Recorded as a standing guardrail in plans/archive/COLLISION_AVOIDANCE_TRACK.md, because the day partition excluded this material for free by keying paragraph counts on enriched dates while a stream framing does not.
- **Reason:** Enforcing it changed no Step 6 gate quantity (3 forced pairings, 100% beyond the +/-1-day window, 51 no-insertion sessions, same verdict), which is the robustness check; it changes denominators only -- axis sessions 1,316 -> 1,132, unclaimed sessions 263 -> 79, no-insertion 51/1,132 (4.5%), annual inventories 12/1,034 (1.2%). Upstream is already safe and was verified rather than assumed: metrics_local_inventory_ceiling and landmark_density_eval's date_sequence both join paragraph_count on enriched_date, so the 11,644 day ceiling, finding C's 14,258 and Step 1's segment ceilings never included post-edition material (3189's 1,579 paragraphs in finding C's table is exactly its in-scope count). The trap is not hypothetical: 3189's post-edition sessions are trailing and bracket geometry isolated them anyway, but 4562's 4 sit inside ordinary brackets where they could be offered to a sitting dated years earlier -- the regression test forces such a pairing at >900 days. Step 5's pending bound re-derivation is the next thing that would hit this: a naive per-inventory paragraph sum would hand 3189 3,858 paragraphs instead of 1,579.

## 2026-09-28: Step 3 gate: pooled-derangement control clears no_data, verdict PASS

- **Context:** scripts/d1c_idf_band_order_eval.py's Step 3 gate (plans/archive/COLLISION_AVOIDANCE_TRACK.md) came back no_data 2026-09-25: real common-band tau 0.809 vs control 0.103, but the single fixed-derangement control only produced 26 common-band pairs against the fixed minimum of 50, so the margin was unmeasured.
- **Decision:** Pool 4 independent fixed-offset cyclic-shift derangements (offsets 1146/2292/3438/4584 of 5730 tier-1 pairs) into one control sample instead of one. Re-ran scripts/d1c_idf_band_order_eval.py: control common-band pairs 26 -> 96 (clears MIN_PAIRS_PER_BAND=50). New verdict: PASS -- common-band tau 0.809 clears the 0.40 floor, is >= 0.75x the rare-band tau (0.4675), and beats the pooled control (0.1736) by margin 0.6354 (threshold 0.20). Step 3 (entity-mention unit-cost edit alignment, structural no-insertion constraint) is now cleared to build; not started this session.
- **Reason:** A single derangement starves the common band because a wrong pairing shares >=2 common-band mentions far less often than a real one (mean entity containment 0.295), so 26/5730 pairs qualified -- a sample-size artifact of the control construction, not evidence about order. Each pooled offset is an independently wrong pairing (no index maps to itself under any offset, verified by test), not reused data, so pooling grows the control sample without inventing evidence. Per-pair display columns in the output dataset still use only the first derangement, for readability; only the gate's aggregate statistics use the pooled set.

## 2026-09-28: Step 3 entity-mention alignment first experiment — closes negative

- **Context:** Built complete first experiment: added enriched-advance DP move (unit cost, symmetric with match/skip), added word-boundary vocab matching, added matched control via segment_gap (identical chains, pure even split). Measured on 21 gold S-code days, 52 open-gap resolutions across 9 gaps. Coverage improved from 38.5% to 86.5%. Result: entity-DP exact agreement 0.2308 vs control 0.5385 on projected subset (n=39) — the aligner scores substantially WORSE than pure segment_gap interpolation.
- **Decision:** Close Step 3. The entity-mention alignment does not beat the no-alignment baseline on a fair comparison. The measured signal is not there.
- **Reason:** Two independent mechanisms (the missing enriched-run DP move and vocabulary word-boundaries) both produced substantial improvements in coverage, but the final accuracy is negative: word-boundary matching recovered real projections (20→45 resolutions) but 73% of the new projections are positional name-substitution (not entity evidence), placing 23 of 39 by >6 paragraphs. A second lever (graded substitution cost per the plan) is explicitly gated on this first experiment beating the null, and it does not. Further work on the entity-stream framing is not motivated by this path.

## 2026-09-29: s6b_known_point_ledger rebuilt on production's own anchors; open-gap population shrinks but remains the majority of unplaced work

- **Context:** anchor_set_reconciliation.py (2026-09-28) found only 65.7% agreement and 62.9% exact-position agreement between alignment_1626_1630's stored tier1_anchor rows (flat-resolution granularity, score>=2.0, consumed by s6b_known_point_ledger.py's known-point chain and Step 3's align_entity_mentions experiment) and production's own live align_session anchors (paragraph-axis granularity, any entity_match_score>0) -- so the 947-open-gap/70.7%-unplaced constraint baseline described a stricter, analysis-only anchor problem than production's segment_day actually solves. User decision recorded the same day (state.json task decision_2026_09_28, never propagated to active_focus/next_actions): rebuild (1), remeasure (2), reassess (3).
- **Decision:** Rebuilt scripts/s6b_known_point_ledger.py's main() to compute interior known points from production's own live align_session + build_axis_overlap call per day (new production_points() function, PRODUCTION_ANCHOR kind), replacing the stored tier1_anchor read entirely. tier1_points()/TIER1 stay defined and unchanged -- s6b_anchor_harvest.py group A and s6d_entity_alignment_gap_eval.py's Step 3 chain read them directly from alignment_1626_1630 and are unaffected by this rebuild. data_manifest.toml's s6b_known_point_ledger entry updated (parent alignment_1626_1630 -> paragraph_axis_1626_1630, description rewritten). 2 new tests added (14/14 passing in the file; full suite 598 passed/10 skipped). Corpus-wide re-run (1626-1630, ~3s): pinned share 37.8% -> 44.1% (5,111 -> 5,966 of 13,530 resolutions), open gaps 947 -> 795, resolutions held in open gaps 5,302 -> 3,827, open-gap share of unplaced 70.7% -> 57.4%.
- **Reason:** Confirms the reconciliation diagnostic's practical consequence: production's looser, paragraph-axis-granularity anchor genuinely pins more resolutions and leaves a smaller, more tractable open-gap population than the stricter stored tier1_anchor ever showed. The open-gap population shrinks by ~28% in absolute resolutions (5,302->3,827) but still holds the majority (57.4%) of nominally-unplaced work, so the track's core open-gap-placement problem is real but smaller than previously measured, not solved or dissolved. Step 3's own negative result (entity-DP exact=0.2308 vs control=0.5385) is UNCHANGED by this rebuild: s6d_entity_alignment_gap_eval.py builds its own chain directly via tier1_points, independent of this ledger's output file, and was scored against gold rather than against this ledger's aggregate stats. Whether to re-run the Step 3 harness against the corrected production-anchor backbone, or pursue a different strategy for the smaller-but-still-majority open population, is the next open decision and is not made here.

## 2026-09-29: Fingerprint anchors audited against sitting assignments, not promoted to truth

- **Context:** The fingerprint match is content-verified flat HTR session → indexed raw HTR session, not enriched sitting → archival date or resolution start. Using its indexed raw date to rewrite enriched assignments would silently treat a potentially wrong session/date label as ground truth.
- **Decision:** Register and run `scripts/fingerprint_assignment_audit.py` as a read-only Step 4 prerequisite. It joins current concordance sitting claims through uniquely fingerprint-matched flat sessions to raw session IDs and compares raw index date labels, preserving unverified and reused cases. Output (`fingerprint_assignment_audit`): 1,123 sitting/session claims; 682 label agreements, 8 label conflicts, 82 reused-raw-session claims and 351 unmatched fingerprints. No mapping or placement changed. Review conflicts before considering any correction; no automatic adoption of the raw date.
- **Reason:** Session identity evidence and sitting-to-session membership are different claims. A disagreement is a candidate for independent review, not a reason to swap a session or move a resolution.

## 2026-09-29: TF-IDF similarity separates correct from wrong enriched<->flat pairings, unlike the entity-bag score

- **Context:** align_session's entity-bag score is flat between correct and false-positive pairs (nw_matches_gt_rate 0.957 vs 0.962, PLAN.md Hypothesis). AlignmentEmbedder's TF-IDF backend was wired into align_session via semantic_weight/similarity_matrix but never run with use_embeddings=True against real data -- alignment_1626_1630.parquet's own semantic_similarity column is 0.0 on all 13,342 rows.
- **Decision:** Measured scripts/tfidf_similarity_pair_discrimination_eval.py on a seeded stratified sample of 150 tier1_anchor days (892 positive pairs) against a within-day pooled-derangement control (2190 negative pairs, scripts/d1c_idf_band_order_eval.py's control pattern reused): positive mean/median similarity 0.180/0.178 vs negative 0.069/0.067, AUC (common-language effect size) 0.9555, same-day top-1 accuracy 0.9518. TF-IDF similarity discriminates a correct enriched<->flat resolution pairing from a same-day wrong one by a real margin, consistently across all five main inventories (per-inventory AUC 0.945-0.967). This is a standalone measurement only -- align_session's semantic_weight/use_embeddings defaults are unchanged and no production wiring was done.
- **Reason:** Closes the untested gap the user flagged: a second, already-built content-comparison mechanism had never been gold-or-control evaluated, unlike phrase matching / entity-order alignment / the LLM judge, all of which have a recorded verdict.

## 2026-09-29: Step 0 gate: TF-IDF margin survives paragraph-granularity transition

- **Context:** Measured AUC on 150 sampled days with enriched-vs-paragraph scoring
- **Decision:** PASS
- **Reason:** AUC 0.893 >= 0.70 threshold. Positive mean 0.189/median 0.200 vs negative 0.098/0.101. Top-1 accuracy 0.468 (correct resolution's best paragraph beats others on same day). Threshold 0.35 still inert (20th percentile of positives); proceed to wiring and calibration in Step 1.

## 2026-09-29: Phase 1b: TF-IDF semantic similarity improves lumped F1 on gold days, plateau at weight ≥0.5

- **Context:** Phase 1b gold-day sweep: TF-IDF backend was wired into s4_corpus_paragraph_predictions.py and s4_paragraph_axis_baseline.py (Phase 1a, already complete), accepting `--semantic_weight` and `--semantic_backend` flags. Tested weights [0.0, 0.5, 1.0, 1.5, 2.0, 3.0, 5.0] on 50 gold days.
- **Decision:** Accept semantic_weight=0.5 for corpus-scale testing (Phase 1c). Lumped F1@tol150 improved from 0.8123 (baseline) to 0.8235 (+0.0112), exceeding Phase 1b success criterion (≥0.01 improvement). All weights ≥0.5 plateau at 0.8235, indicating rapid saturation. Secondary improvements consistent across all tolerances: lumped F1@tol0 +0.0113, lumped F1@tol50 +0.0112. Coverage unchanged (19/21 eligible days predicted). Proceed to Phase 1c: corpus-scale run with semantic_weight=0.5 to measure impact on global separation accuracy, coverage, and span metrics.
- **Reason:** The improvement, modest in absolute terms (+1.1pp), is consistent across tolerances and passes the predefined success gate. Saturation at low weight (0.5) suggests the semantic signal is fully captured without overweighting. Phase 1a wiring (corpus predictor and gold-day harness) was already complete, so moving to corpus scale is immediately tractable. A corpus-wide run is necessary to answer whether the gold-day gain holds at 1,240+ days and whether it moves the headline Global-primary ceiling metric (currently 68.8% of ceiling, 8,015/11,644 separated).

## 2026-09-29: Phase 1c: Corpus-scale semantic similarity run—consistent but modest improvement

- **Context:** Ran `uv run python -m scripts.s4_corpus_paragraph_predictions --semantic_backend tfidf --semantic_weight 0.5` on full 1626-1630 corpus (1,594 calendar days, 1,121 predictions + 473 abstained missing_htr). Rebuilt `resolution_concordance_1626_1630` to compute corpus-wide separation metrics.
- **Decision:** Accept semantic_weight=0.5 as production default for the corpus predictor. Separated resolutions improved from 8,015 (68.8% of ceiling) to 8,084 (69.4% of ceiling), a gain of +69 resolutions (+0.9%). The improvement is consistent with gold-day findings but smaller in magnitude at corpus scale.
- **Reason:** The +0.9% gain at corpus scale, though modest, validates the gold-day finding: semantic similarity helps alignment, but its leverage depends on the resolution type and paragraph context. The cost is negligible (corpus predictor runtime unchanged; semantic_weight=0.5 saturates the signal per Phase 1b, no need for higher weights). The consistency (gold-day +1.1pp → corpus +0.6pp in % of ceiling) suggests the effect is real but varying by date/resolution, not overfitting to gold days. Global-primary criterion (≥5,822 separated) remains met with margin; corpus metrics are stable. Recommend wiring semantic_weight=0.5 as default in build_alignment_new.py for ongoing work, but do NOT prioritize it as a breakthrough improvement — the baseline (68.8%) was already strong, and the delta is at the margin of measurement noise for a 1,300-day corpus.

## 2026-09-30: Pinned-but-wrong session mapping: open, not closed by Step 6

- **Context:** plans/archive/COLLISION_AVOIDANCE_TRACK.md Step 6 (2026-09-25) closed 'is every enriched sitting mapped to some HTR session' (yes, 1,089/1,089 in the five annual inventories) but explicitly left 'is a pinned mapping correct' untested, naming a cheap follow-up (compare pinned enriched date against s6b_session_fingerprint_match's content-verified raw date, not the flat label) that was never run. Step 6's own status line reads 'GATE DID NOT PASS' / done, which is easy to misread as the whole area being settled. 2026-09-30 found a concrete instance: 1630-02-22 (inventory 3189, part of the fully-pinned 108/108 population, resolution_source=ledger_direct, inventory_ambiguous=False -- no existing confidence signal flags it) has 28 enriched resolutions against a 12-paragraph axis, with 7 resolutions carrying zero entity-overlap anchor evidence anywhere in the axis, one-sided against the day's edge -- the shape a mis-mapped boundary produces, not axis coarseness (a second zero-anchor run on the same day IS bracketed by real anchors on both sides, and reads as ordinary coarseness by contrast).
- **Decision:** Do not treat Step 6 as having closed the mapping-correctness question. Keep the named cheap follow-up (fingerprint-date vs label-date comparison for pinned sittings) open and blocked pending someone running it, independent of Step 6's own step-level status. Additionally: any future correction mechanism built on confirmed cross-day anchors must resolve how many distinct upstream sessions are compressed in each flanking group before reassigning a count to a neighboring day -- a cut point plus a resolution count is not sufficient, because the flanking group can itself be lumped from more than one true session.
- **Reason:** A step's closing verdict only closes the specific question it gated on. A caveat inside a closed step that the step's own text says is unresolved does not inherit the step's 'done' status, and nothing was forcing that distinction to survive independent of the track doc's prose until this entry. Recorded separately so a future session checking docs/DECISIONS.md sees this as open without having to re-read all of Step 6's prose first.

## 2026-09-30: Record retired steps in three parts, not as a bare verdict

- **Context:** plans/*.md are full of bare step verdicts -- 'NEGATIVE', 'GATE DID NOT PASS' -- which PLAN.md then compresses further into one line per step. Two failures followed from that this week. (a) Step 6 of COLLISION_AVOIDANCE_TRACK closed as 'GATE DID NOT PASS' while its own text left the pinned-but-wrong mapping question explicitly open and unbuilt; the open caveat was invisible under the step's done status for five days until a concrete instance turned up by an unrelated route. (b) The per-rank TF-IDF wiring was recorded 'NEGATIVE globally (best -25 separated)'; scoring the same condition for correctness against gold on 2026-09-30 found it IMPROVES placement (exact start agreement 0.5032 -> 0.5865 applied everywhere, better than the gated variant's 0.5321). The verdict was accurate against separated_count and misleading as a summary, because coverage and correctness disagree in sign on solid days.
- **Decision:** Record a retired step as three parts, per the 'Retired step' definition added to docs/ITERATION_POLICY.md: (1) what the gate said now and against WHICH metric; (2) what it established that survives the verdict -- mechanism confirmed, population sized, constraint measured, premise overturned; (3) the conditions under which it should be re-evaluated. Applied retroactively to COLLISION_AVOIDANCE_TRACK Step 6 and to TFIDF_EMBEDDINGS_TRACK wiring 2. Separately: an unresolved caveat inside a retired step does not inherit that step's done status and gets its own decision entry.
- **Reason:** A retired step is sometimes a wrong idea but often a right idea at the wrong time, which may evaluate differently against a future evolved state of the system. A bare NEGATIVE destroys the information needed to recognise when conditions have changed enough to revisit it -- as the TF-IDF case demonstrates, where the step was negative only against the metric it happened to be gated on, and positive against the one the project ultimately cares about.

## 2026-10-01: Pinned-but-wrong session mapping, sized -- small for date identity, but does not explain 1630-02-22

- **Context:** Ran the cheap follow-up named 2026-09-25 and reaffirmed open 2026-09-30: `scripts/fingerprint_assignment_audit.py` (already written, registered, never executed) joins every pinned sitting's `resolved_session_id` through `s6b_session_fingerprint_match` (content-fingerprint, independent of resolutions_flat's own numbering) to `session_index_all`'s raw, archive-derived `session_date`, and compares that against the enriched date. 1,123 pinned sitting/session claims: 682 (60.7%) `date_label_agrees`, 8 (0.7%) `date_label_conflict` (confirmed wrong -- e.g. `1629-03-18` claims `session-3188-num-47` whose content-verified raw session is dated `1629-02-19`, 27 days off), 82 (7.3%) `raw_session_reused` (the matched raw session is itself claimed by 2-3 different enriched dates -- 40 distinct raw sessions behind the 82 rows, consistent with `COLLISION_AVOIDANCE_TRACK`'s already-known "67 sessions claimed by 2+ sittings", not a new anomaly), 351 (31.3%) `unmatched_fingerprint` (no unique raw-session match, so no comparison possible). Separately, the 9 session-date-drift candidates `plans/archive/STAGED_ANCHOR_CONSOLIDATION.md` Step 2 surfaced via raw ES `session_date` (itself uncompared against this project's own fingerprint standard, by that doc's own caveat): 5 of 6 checked here come back `date_label_agrees` once compared the right way -- the apparent drift was flat-session numbering offset (+1, +2, +13, -2), not a date problem. And the concrete instance that motivated this whole question, `1630-02-22`, itself comes back `date_label_agrees`, zero offset, zero delta.
- **Decision:** Close guardrail 1's circularity concern for the **date-identity** failure mode: the pinned-but-wrong population is small (8/1,123 = 0.7% confirmed; 7.3% ambiguous via known multi-claim collisions, not new wrongness) and does not block treating `session_boundary`/D as the first, most-trusted channel in `CHANNEL_WEIGHT` order at the corpus scale Step 1 of the procedure needs. Do **not** treat this as resolving `1630-02-22` or the pigeonhole-collapse shape that raised the original worry -- that day's session is correctly dated and correctly content-identified; its problem (28 resolutions, 12 paragraphs, a one-sided zero-anchor pile) is a different failure mode entirely, belonging to Steps 3/4 (partial abstention, anchor-channel wiring), not to session-date mapping. Leave the 351 `unmatched_fingerprint` rows and 82 `raw_session_reused` rows as known-unverifiable / known-collision respectively, not as additional pinned-but-wrong mass.
- **Reason:** The named follow-up answers exactly one question -- is the pinned session's *calendar date* right -- and answers it cleanly: mostly yes, rarely (confirmably) no. Conflating that with `1630-02-22`'s crowding problem would have misdirected effort toward re-auditing session dates when the actual defect is upstream of date mapping entirely (anchor supply/placement within an already-correctly-dated session). Keeping the two apart is the same discipline the 2026-09-30 "record retired steps in three parts" entry asks for: a gate closes the specific thing it tested, not the whole area a step's title suggests.

## 2026-10-01: Partial abstention in segment_day Step 3 — gate PASS; abstained population was disproportionately wrong

- **Context:** plans/archive/STAGED_ANCHOR_CONSOLIDATION.md Step 3 targeted the "fabricated precision" of segment_day's today behavior: when a gap has more unanchored resolutions than paragraphs of axis room (pigeonhole_floor > 1), the gap is arithmetically unseparable, yet today segment_day still calls segment_gap and forces repeats, producing illusory precision on dates like 1630-02-22 (28 resolutions, 12 paragraphs, one-sided zero-anchor pile). The honest output for that day should be: place the 7 anchored resolutions at their paragraphs, abstain entirely on the 2 forced-collapse gaps (16 resolutions unresolved), place the 3 gap-fillable resolutions via segment_gap in the floor==1 gaps.
- **Decision:** Implemented partial abstention: `segment_day` now returns `list[int | None]` where `None` marks positions unresolved because their gap was forced (floor > 1). Callers filter `None` before building boundary entries (s4_corpus_paragraph_predictions.py, s4_paragraph_axis_baseline.py). **Gap-mass gate:** seed day 1630-02-22 has exactly 2 abstained gaps (16 resolutions), matching expected; other seed days have adequate room. **Gold correctness (Guardrail 5), corrected:** a first measurement attempt (follow-up commit `fc89c0b`) reported "0.5080 before and after, no change" by reading `resolution_concordance_1626_1630`'s cached parquet for both conditions — but that file's mtime (2026-09-29) predates this step's code change (2026-10-01) by two days, so both "before" and "after" read the identical, un-regenerated, pre-abstention production run. That result was invalid and has been replaced here, not merely appended to. Re-measured independently by calling `align_session` + `segment_day` live per gold day (old-behavior reconstructed by always calling `segment_gap`, matching pre-Step-3 code exactly; new behavior is the current `segment_day`), scored against gold the same way `separated_accuracy_eval.accuracy()` does (dropna on both predicted and gold columns), across all 50 `boundary_gold_sample` days (13 skipped, no axis): **OLD exact-agreement 0.5431 (189/348 scoreable) vs NEW 0.6486 (179/276 scoreable)**. Restricted to exactly the 72 gold-scoreable resolutions NEW abstains on, **OLD's exact-agreement on that subset was only 0.1389 (10/72)** — far below its own 0.5431 baseline. **Gate PASS on both gap-mass and correctness**, with correctness improving, not neutral.
- **Reason:** Guardrail 5 requires measuring correctness for any placement change, not just placement-improvement claims — abstaining on resolutions is a placement change by definition. The corrected measurement shows the abstained population (119 resolutions across 18 of 50 gold days) was not a neutral or random subset: where gold could score it, the pre-abstention DP was right only 13.9% of the time versus its 54.3% overall baseline, confirming these were exactly the fabricated-precision forced-repeat guesses the step's motivation described, not borderline-correct placements being sacrificed for honesty. The NEW exact-agreement figure (0.6486) is on a smaller, easier-to-score denominator (276 vs 348) — some of that rise is the ordinary effect of dropping hard cases, not a free improvement on a fixed population, and should not be read as "the same resolutions are now scored better." The load-bearing number is the 0.1389-vs-0.5431 contrast: it is evidence the abstained set was genuinely low-quality, which is what justifies treating abstention as a correctness-safe (here, correctness-positive) change rather than a cost accepted for honesty's sake alone. Separately: the invalid first attempt is itself a process finding — any future before/after correctness check on this codebase must verify the comparison is driven by code actually re-run under both conditions, not by re-reading a cached corpus dataset whose mtime was never checked against the code change being evaluated.

## 2026-10-02: Group B anchors deferred from Step 4a/4b: measured confusion, not signal, where it would matter

- **Context:** plans/archive/STAGED_ANCHOR_CONSOLIDATION.md Step 4a/4b needs Groups B/C/D attributed to specific enriched resolutions; Group B (entity_surface_matches, fuzzy_surface_scan) is harvested axis-paragraph-keyed with no enriched_index, unlike Group A's existing (enriched_id, axis_id) overlap table. Rather than inventing new name-matching to attribute B anchors, scripts/s6b_group_b_signal_check.py (new, diagnostic only) reused the project's own place/org/per_overlap_1626_1630 overlap data as the sole attribution arbiter (exact normalized canonical_name match against that paragraph's already-resolved entity names), scored on the 35 of 50 boundary_gold_sample days that also appear in s6b_anchor_harvest.jsonl.
- **Decision:** Do not wire Group B into Step 4a/4b via this attribution mechanism. Scope Step 4a/4b to Groups A (live, per Step 2 finding) + C (already partially wired via position_scores) + D (structural backbone). Keep Group B deferred, now for a measured reason.
- **Reason:** Of 6,174 Group B rows across 35 gold days, only 9.6% (594) attribute cleanly to one enriched resolution via the existing overlap data; of those, gold-scoreable rows agree with gold 65.3% of the time overall -- but that figure is dominated by cells Group A already anchors (75.4% of scored rows), where Group B is merely redundant confirmation. On the 24.6% of scored rows where Group A has NO existing anchor at that cell -- the only population Step 4 would actually be using Group B to change a placement -- exact agreement drops to 28.2% (31/110), well below the corpus's current ~65% baseline. Group B is confusion exactly where it would matter, not signal; this is a measured finding (not the original docstring's unverified deferral), re-evaluate only if a better attribution mechanism than exact-name-match against the existing overlap data is built.

## 2026-10-02: Group C anchors deferred from Step 4a/4b too: hard attribution repeats Group B's failure

- **Context:** Step 4a's staging spec requires a channel to produce committed `(enriched_index, axis_index)` anchors to shrink gaps for the next stage. Group C (`s2_anchor_phrases`, `s4_opening_phrase_candidates`) is axis-paragraph-keyed with no enriched-resolution index -- the same shape as Group B. `scripts/s6c_group_c_attribution_check.py` (new, diagnostic only) attributed each phrase hit inside a `pigeonhole_floor == 1` gap to the resolution rank whose even-split "ideal" position (the same baseline `segment_gap` already scores every candidate against) sits closest to it -- a positional claim reusing existing `segment_gap` machinery, not a new name-matching mechanism -- then compared the resulting hard placement against (a) no evidence at all and (b) today's existing soft `position_scores` + `snap_to_phrase` wiring, on the 50-day `boundary_gold_sample`.
- **Decision:** Do not give Group C a hard-anchor role via positional attribution. Keep it exactly where it already works: a soft `position_scores` bonus inside gaps Group A already anchors, plus the existing `snap_to_phrase` nudge. Scope Step 4a/4b to Groups A + D only.
- **Reason:** Of 94 attributed, gold-scoreable ranks, hard attribution scored 30.9% exact agreement vs. 47.9% for both the no-evidence baseline and today's existing soft wiring (identical to each other in this population) -- hard attribution disagreed with today's output on 41/94 ranks, fixing 5 and breaking 21. The attribution rule is static (no new evidence arrives between a "soft" and "hard" read of it, so repeating it cannot correct it) and is wrong more often than right exactly where it would change anything -- the same shape as Group B's measured confusion above. User follow-up (2026-10-02): a phrase hit may still carry real value as a local boundary-*existence* signal, decoupled from resolution identity, inside an already-forced-abstention gap -- not measured here, parked as the next step (PLAN.md).

## 2026-10-02: TF-IDF diagonal relaxation isolated: real but modest and local-only; a text-lookup bug fixed along the way

- **Context:** Step 4b's "relax `anchor_only_diagonal` to accept any channel clearing a threshold" turned out to already be shipped -- the gate's own `has_anchor` check (`build_alignment_new.py:938`) accepts `sem_sim >= min_semantic_threshold` regardless of entity overlap, and `semantic_weight=0.5` has been the production default since 2026-09-29 (+69 resolutions / +0.9% corpus-wide). `scripts/s6c_semantic_anchor_isolation_check.py` (new) classified every backbone anchor under that production config into `entity_anchor` (would exist without semantic help), `semantic_new_anchor` (entity_match_score == 0, exists only via the semantic branch), or `gap_filled`, scored against `boundary_gold_sample`'s exact-start-paragraph convention -- a different metric than Phase 1b/1c's lumped-F1@tolerance, and the first time this channel's *hard-anchor* contribution specifically (as opposed to its aggregate effect) was measured.
- **Bug found and fixed:** first pass found zero `semantic_new_anchor` cells across all 50 gold days and `sem_sim` values of ~0.001-0.003 -- two orders of magnitude below this channel's own measured noise floor (0.0201 margin, 2026-09-29 entry above). Traced to `s4_paragraph_axis_baseline.py`'s own `load_enriched_texts_by_date()`: it keyed enriched text by `volgnr`/`#{resolution_index}`, while its caller looked it up by `day["enriched_ids"]`'s XML-filename ids (e.g. `162915okt.xml#0`) -- three incompatible ID schemes that never matched, so every lookup silently fell back to a placeholder string (`f"enriched_{enriched_id}"`) and all gold-day semantic similarity (today's diagnostics, and apparently Phase 1b's own calibration sweep that chose `semantic_weight=0.5`) had been computed against that placeholder, not real resolution text. **Fixed:** `load_enriched_texts_by_date()` now returns texts ordered positionally by `resolution_index`, mirroring `scripts/s4_corpus_paragraph_predictions.py`'s already-correct implementation of the same function name, matched positionally rather than by ID string. Verified this bug did **not** affect the corpus-wide production script or the TFIDF_EMBEDDINGS_TRACK's own measurement scripts (`s6c_gated_correctness_eval.py`, `s6c_similarity_position_scores_eval.py`, `s6c_similarity_rank_scores_eval.py`), which all already import the positionally-correct version -- only `s4_paragraph_axis_baseline.py`'s own gold-day `main()` was affected. Phase 1b's calibration sweep ran via this same buggy script, so that calibration's numeric justification is suspect even though Phase 1c's corpus-wide deployment of the resulting weight was computed on real text and is unaffected.
- **Decision:** Keep `semantic_weight=0.5` as production default; no change to shipped behavior.
