- **Stage 5's script-retirement half has no dependency on Stage 4** and could be its own, smaller session — only the `build_brackets`-as-graph-adapter piece of Stage 5 is gated on Stage 4 holding.
- **Nothing committed.** 44 files dirty across this session and the prior one. The honest state is: a verified, working spine with no placement-affecting change anywhere in it.

## 2026-10-09 (later the same day): Stage 4 first pass built and run once; a second design gap found and left open

- **The four decisions above were answered by the user before any code.** (1) `MENTIONS` row shape checked live against the three overlap tables — raw match-candidate rows, no precomputed score; (2) cost fitting deferred, mechanism built first on the existing production overlap score as an *unfitted* signal; (3) first population is the clean bracket `1629-03-02` (`k_e=k_f=5`), not the `3187` 305→320 stress case; (4) scored standalone, not against `align_session`/`align_entity_mentions`/`segment_gap`. `sequence_graph/align.py` implements all four named defect fixes; `tests/test_sequence_graph_align.py` (11 tests, synthetic, no corpus access) passes.
- **One correction needed mid-build, not just porting the spec:** `insertion_cost` cannot share `match_cost`'s formula, or `match(a) + insertion(b) >= insertion(b)` always holds and the DP never chooses MATCH-then-SPLIT over skipping ahead to a fresh match — `split_discount` fixes this.
- **The first real run surfaced a genuine limitation, not a bug, then a bug.** On `1629-03-02`, only 2 of 5 enriched/HTR pairs carry any entity-overlap evidence at all (checked the raw matrix directly — this day is short and administrative). The first cut of "free end gaps" let the DP discard an *unbounded* trailing run for free once an early quiet stretch made bailing out cheaper, discarding a genuine match (ref3/read3, overlap 1.027). Fixed same day: free leading/trailing status is evidence-gated (zero overlap against every ref item), not positional — one item with real evidence ends the free run from that point on. Verified: the discarded match now survives; total cost moved from 4.916 to the correct 5.489 (matching the hand-derived alternative-path cost from the first run).
- **Tested on a spread of real days (user's explicit ask), not just the clean one.** 7 gold days from clean to severe over/under-segmentation (`diff_3plus`, up to `k_f=47` vs `k_e=14`) — no crashes, costs and separated-counts scale plausibly. Found and fixed one real bug along the way: `1626-02-28`'s gold boundaries include `unit: "out_of_range"` entries with no `flat_id` (gold genuinely could not place them); the scoring adapter assumed every boundary carries one and raised `KeyError`.
- **Result on the one scored bracket, standalone per decision 4, not yet a gate:** `1629-03-02`, abstained=False, total_cost=5.489, 3/5 gold exact agreement, 1/5 `separated` (uniform-null start positions `[0,1,2,3,4]`). Not compared against `align_session`'s 0.957/0.962 or `segment_gap`'s 0.628 — that comparison is still deferred.
- **A second design gap, raised after this first pass and NOT yet resolved or locked into the plan:** `align.py`'s move set (`MATCH`/`DELETION_GAP`/`INSERTION_SPLIT`/`SOFT_CLIP`) has no dedicated handling for (a) nihil-actum ref days — should these take a distinct `SKIP` move rather than going through ordinary `DELETION_GAP`/`MATCH`? (b) `DATED_AS`'s `strict`/`bootstrap` channels — should these be solver-visible edge subtypes rather than an opaque `Evidence.channel` string? (c) how Track P's `CONTAINS` refinement (splitting a `Paragraph`) interacts with `ALIGNED_TO` identity once sub-paragraph division lands; (d) whether the move set should forbid HTR-side insertion outright rather than pricing it via `INSERTION_SPLIT`/`SOFT_CLIP`. None of the four are decided; the plan file's own "next session's short list" (cost fitting, the three-mechanism benchmark) did not name these. Flagged so the gap is visible before Stage 4 is extended past this one bracket, not discovered by a gate failure later.
- **Still nothing committed to git at the point this was found.**

- **Context:** review's cost-first heuristic (COST_BONUS: cheap=5, session=2, multi-session=0) ranks archival-provenance above sequence-graph because sequence-graph is multi-session and already has metrics recorded (losing the no-metric +2 bonus), even though sequence-graph is the active live work and its open Stage-4 move-set questions (nihil framing, DATED_AS subtypes, Track P granularity, forbid-insertion) are mid-discussion with the user.

## 2026-10-09: all four Stage 4 move-set design questions resolved, live with the user

The four open items from the previous entry ("a second design gap... NOT yet resolved") are now settled:

1. **Nihil-actum: not a `SKIP` move; a checkable invariant, and the live data refines the framing further than expected.** User's correction: a nihil-actum day is a milestone (a session exists, with no resolutions), not a gap needing a special transition. Checked directly in `notebooks/sequence_graph_exploration.ipynb` §7: every one of the 127 nihil-actum dates in `running_axis` has `k_e == 1` (the formulaic "nihil actum" entry is itself one enriched resolution, not zero) and **none** currently resolve to an HTR session. So the live shape is "exactly one formulaic resolution, no pinned session," not yet "session exists, zero resolutions" — no code change follows from this today; it is recorded as a refinement for whenever this becomes an actual Stage 4 move-set change, not silently smoothed over.
2. **`DATED_AS` channel stays upstream-only.** Decided, not revisited: folding `strict`/`bootstrap` parse-confidence into `align.py`'s per-bracket cost would double-count uncertainty the bracket-construction/monotonicity gate already prices in.
3. **Track P unifies with `ALIGNED_TO` at paragraph granularity.** Decided: the read sequence should eventually be `Paragraph`/`CONTAINS`-child level, not `HtrResolution`-level, so a future sub-paragraph split is a finer read item the same DP already handles rather than a second alignment pass. Not yet implemented -- `align.py` still operates on `flat_id`s; this is a design decision for when Track P's P3 (not yet implemented either) and Stage 4 meet.
4. **"Forbid HTR insertion in the move set" clarified, and it was never about forbidding a move type.** User's actual point: unlike Levenshtein-style edit distance, an HTR resolution can never be relocated out of its position in the flat stream -- there is no "insert move" that moves content around; `align_bracket`'s DP already cannot express reordering (only `(i+1,j+1)`/`(i+1,j)`/`(i,j+1)` transitions exist, so the read index never decreases). This was already true by construction and unverified; now pinned down by a new regression test (`test_read_items_are_never_reordered_only_matched_split_or_dropped`, `tests/test_sequence_graph_align.py`, 12/12 passing) and stated in `align_bracket`'s own docstring. **Carried caveat, not resolved away:** the "fixed order" the DP trusts is itself an interpretation of HTR region detection (heading / page number / attendance list / marginalia vs. body text have no single canonical reading order on the page, and region detection can mis-order or mis-classify them). The project treats the resulting flat-stream order as fixed for all practical purposes; a genuine region-detection error in some bracket is a separate upstream defect to flag and fix at the source, never something the move set should try to route around.

**No further Stage 4 design work is blocked.** Next, per the user's explicit ordering (2026-10-09): scope cost-fitting via log-probabilities (`match_cost` → `-log P(match | overlap)`, `insertion_cost` → `-log P(split | overlap)`, `gap_cost` → `-log P(deletion)`, `soft_clip_cost` → `-log P(contamination)`, replacing `CostModel`'s hand-set constants) -- this is `TRACK_S_ANCHOR_SHIFTING.md`'s own open question ("the pipeline is already probabilistic and merely uncalibrated"), and needs its own calibration population (gold matches/splits/deletions/contamination, built the same way the 0.9555 TF-IDF AUC check used true pairs vs. within-day derangement negatives) before any constant changes -- not a quick edit to the defaults.

- **The fit changed `match_cost`'s formula, not just its constants, per the plan agreed before building.** `CostModel.match_cost` now takes `-log(sigmoid(intercept + coef*overlap))` (`softplus(-z)`) when `match_logit` is set; the original `base - lambda*overlap` stays the default (`match_logit=None`) so no existing test or run changed. `gap_cost` needed no formula change, only a better number: `-log(deletion_rate)`, deletion_rate measured empirically (0.421, an upper-bound proxy -- "gold does not locate it" conflates true deletion with "not annotated", the same caveat `add_aligned_to_gold` already carries).

Named as the next concrete blocker, not fixed here.

## 2026-10-09: a worked example from the user re-derives staged-anchor-consolidation's own design, and names a genuinely new piece

User's worked example (three hard-anchored sessions, a known session k_e giving decaying positional plausibility away from an anchor, a weak entity match "accumulating on top of" that prior, and sub-paragraph splitting to make the counts fit) turned out to be, almost exactly, `plans/archive/STAGED_ANCHOR_CONSOLIDATION.md`'s own 5-step procedure (objective anchors -> reconcile -> subdivide -> repeat, written 2026-09-30, never executed) -- independently re-derived, plus one piece that plan only named in principle ("staging raises reliability... weak anchors become safe once staged") without ever specifying the arithmetic. The positional-decay prior *is* that arithmetic, made concrete.

**Clarified why this is not a rerun of an already-failed idea.** The project's two relevant negative findings -- `align_session`'s `anchor_only_diagonal` gate making weak evidence *structurally invisible*, and `segment_gap`'s bonus-vs-dispersion-penalty mechanism swallowing evidence regardless of source -- are both about mechanisms Stage 4's `align_bracket` does not share. It has no hard gate excluding weak overlap from a MATCH move; it only prices it. So testing this idea against `align_bracket` is a genuinely new test.

**Hard-evidence ceiling, decided: structural partition, not a numeric threshold.** User asked for hard evidence to have a threshold soft evidence can never exceed, however much of it accumulates. A numeric cost bound would work but needs re-proving whenever bracket size changes. Recommended instead: hard anchors cut a bracket into independent gaps, and `align_bracket` runs separately inside each gap, never across an anchor boundary -- soft evidence then has no path that could contradict a hard anchor, not just a disadvantaged one. This is exactly what `running_axis.chain`'s `GapLedger`/`build_brackets` already does; it has just never been composed with `align_bracket`.

**Step 1 (measure first, user's explicit ordering) DONE: `sequence_graph/position_prior.py`.** Does NOT touch `CostModel`/`align_bracket` yet -- only asks whether a local uniform-spread expectation predicts a gold resolution's true position well enough to be worth a cost term. Measured on all 344 gold-located resolutions (33 days): median displacement **0** (unbiased), MAD **1.0**, 57.3% within 1 paragraph, 70.3% within 2 -- but std 7.1 and a tail out to -51, driven almost entirely by two already-known severe over-segmentation days (`1626-11-16`, k_e=14 vs k_f=47; `1626-10-27`). **Excluding just those two of ~33 days more than halves the spread** (std 7.1 -> 3.0, within-1 57.3% -> 60.9%, within-2 70.3% -> 75%, range [-51,+6] -> [-12,+6]) -- the tail is a known, nameable pathology class, not generic noise across the corpus. Verdict: **the signal is real and worth building on**, but a single global Gaussian decay would be dominated by the two outlier days; the prior should use robust statistics (median/MAD) or exclude/handle severe-mismatch days separately, not fit one distribution to everything. 3 tests in `tests/test_sequence_graph_position_prior.py`; full suite 906 passed, 10 skipped; `data_io.check`/`scripts/svz.py doctor` clean.

**Next, per the user's ordering:** scope the full multi-session, anchor-bounded chain (per-gap `align_bracket` + the positional-prior cost term, executing `staged-anchor-consolidation`'s procedure with Stage 4's DP as its "reconcile" step) as its own plan addition before any more code -- touches `running_axis.chain` as well as `sequence_graph`, so it needs a proper write-up, not an inline decision. After it is built, the user wants a demonstration on a **randomly selected**, not hand-picked, location in the corpus -- a held-out validation protocol to build into the gate now, not add as an afterthought.

- **`query.py` now loads the saved graph (`sequence_graph.persist.load_graph`) instead of rebuilding** -- `_load()` is a direct read, no `build()`/`add_*` calls left in it. `_load_for_range` adds solver edges transiently in memory for any requested `solver_dates` the saved graph doesn't already have, printing a note that a transient addition is not yet persisted; it does not silently write back on a read. `fit_cost.py`/`position_prior.py` were not touched -- on inspection they never used the graph object at all (they read `boundary_gold_sample` and `day_paragraph_stream` directly), so there was nothing to rewire there despite an earlier (wrong) statement that there would be.
- **What deliberately still rebuilds fresh, not from the saved graph, and why:** `correspondence.py --report`, `classify.py`, `consolidate.py`'s own `main()`, and the entire test suite all still call `build()`/`add_*` directly. These are the construction logic's own regression gates (reproducing `reconcile_sequences.py`'s 323/406/81 exactly, etc.) -- reading a cached graph there would test whether yesterday's save matches itself, not whether today's construction code is still correct.

## 2026-10-12: adjudication “HARD vs PROD folio” is the wrong dating question

- **User:** on 23 Sept, HARD `num-159` scan 386 vs PROD `num-150` scan 357 — but PROD `num-150` is ~1 Sept paper; if hard dates already cover ~28 Aug–22 Sep, that folio **cannot** be 23 Sept.
- **Check:** physical places `num-150` on **1626-09-01**; concordance `resolved_session_id` reuses **`num-150` for 1626-09-23**. Scan 357 is one sitting’s paper, not two. Hard map for 23 Sept is scan 386.
- **User (follow-up):** yes — because concordance **does** use date as a sign. The lag is the artifact of a date-mediated join, not proof of a second sitting on 357.
- **Decision:** Drop HARD-vs-PROD folio as a dating bake-off. Concordance `PINNED_TO` is date-signed; physical is `OBSERVED_ON∘DATED_AS`; fingerprint `ALIGNED_TO` must stay content-only (session neighbourhood heuristic, not day-join). “Prefer production” on day-keyed lag runs mostly rediscovers concordance’s date sign, not an independent content verdict about which folio is day D.

## 2026-10-12: rephrase — what “prefer production” does and does not prove

- **User:** the striking lag result (fingerprint prefers production, not hard) *looks* like proof that corpus-wide session recalibration must come first — but that was not the question. The question was: given session shift in many resolutions, can fingerprinting shift them? That assumes we already know which sessions are where, else sequences cannot be compared.
- **Rephrase (accepted):**
  1. **Research question (ALIGNED_TO):** resolutions sit in the wrong *session slot*; fingerprint should move them to the right slot among a known session sequence.
  2. **Prerequisite:** a usable session geometry (where each sitting’s HTR block is). Without that, “match resolution R to session S±Δ” has no stable comparison space.
  3. **What lag actually measured:** on days where hard dating and production pins disagree, does *day-keyed* enriched text prefer hard’s HTR or production’s HTR? That is a **PINNED_TO / day-join conflict diagnostic**, not a session-shift recovery test.
- **Evaluate:**
  - **Gold** answers (1) under synthetic lag: recovery=1.0 — instrument can pick the gold session vs a Δ competitor when the true pairing is known.
  - **Lag prefer_hard=0** does **not** answer (1). It shows edition text still affiliates with production packing when the day→session lid is contested — expected if enrichment was built on production. It neither proves hard dating wrong nor proves fingerprint cannot shift resolutions once sessions are placed.
  - **“Recalibrate sessions corpus-wide first”** is right as a *dependency* for applying (1) in production (need trusted candidates), wrong as the *reading* of prefer_hard=0 (that number does not measure session-shift success). Dating lids and ALIGNED_TO rematch stay separate; folio adjudication is about lids, not about whether fingerprint works.
- **Next well-posed apply of (1):** hold a session axis fixed (production *or* a reviewed hard map), take resolutions believed shifted relative to *that* axis, rematch among neighbouring sessions — do not use “enriched for calendar day D” as the join when D’s pin is the thing in dispute.

No silent rematch; notes stay out of production until reviewed.

## 2026-10-12: fingerprint session-shift — gold first; preceding-scan parked

- **User:** use fingerprinting for resolution session-shift; ask whether that is a separate track or needs full dating recalibration first; try gold first; also float preceding-scan text to decide which resolution ends a session (raw HTR harder).
- **Decision:** Dating lids (`PINNED_TO*` / hard map) and resolution match (`ALIGNED_TO` / fingerprint) stay **separate tracks** — no corpus-wide dating rewrite before asking whether fingerprint recovers the correct session under lag. **Gold first:** synthetic Δ competitor on held-out tier1 (fit on train days only); gate = gold-session recovery. Lag-window rematch deferred until that gate. **Preceding-scan / session-tail channel deferred** (not part of gold gate): on ambiguous cuts, fingerprint HTR on scans before the next session’s first `OBSERVED_ON` folio to name the closing resolution of the prior sitting — tiers A `paragraph_axis` trail → B archival `scan_ids` (~72%) → C raw HTR.
- **Built:** `sequence_graph.fingerprint_session_shift --on-gold`; dataset `fingerprint_session_shift_proof`; tests `tests/test_fingerprint_session_shift.py`.
- **Run:** `uv run python -m sequence_graph.fingerprint_session_shift --on-gold --write`
- **Measured gold (2026-10-12):** 200 held-out days / 1,182 pairs; primary Δ=8 **gold_session_recovery=1.0** (1032 gold / **0 competitor** / 26 abstain). Instrument PASS under synthetic lag. Persisted as `fingerprint_session_shift_gold` (split from lag after overwrite bug).
- **Measured lag (2026-10-12):** `--on-lag` on `1626-09-21`..`1626-10-25`: 15 disagree days / 182 enriched → **prefer_hard_rate=0.0** (hard 0 / production 153 / abstain 29). Literal opening on hard 15/15; no review claims in window. Persisted as `fingerprint_session_shift_lag`.
- **Channel trust on this conflict (explicit, 2026-10-12):** Two independent channels disagree on the same window — scan/hard path (literal openings 15/15 + `OBSERVED_ON∘DATED_AS`) vs enriched-content fingerprint (sides with production). **Dating lids:** keep hard map as the active dating claim set here (archival heading path); do not let fingerprint prefer-production overturn hard dating. **ALIGNED_TO / content:** do **not** rematch flats onto the hard session from fingerprint alone — edition text still affiliates with production HTR. Interpret as: dating and content association are separate problems on this stretch (enriched may be filed under the wrong day relative to the paper), not as “hard recalibrate DP is wrong because fingerprint said production.” Next coupling is not silent flat rematch (boundary-gold / preceding-scan / which-enriched-on-day).
- **Provenance fix:** `--on-gold` / `--on-lag` previously shared `fingerprint_session_shift_proof` and overwrote each other; split to `fingerprint_session_shift_gold` + `fingerprint_session_shift_lag`.

## 2026-10-12: Break dating↔content deadlock (inherit date + decouple pin promote)

- **Context:** The standing rule “content never writes dating” (`ALIGNED_TO` / fingerprint must not write `PINNED_TO`) combined with the procedural gate “finish content re-align under hard lids, *then* overwrite production `PINNED_TO`” deadlocks the remediation track. Content is forbidden to unlock dating, yet pin promote waited on content finishing. Substantively the same stretch already shows two independent truths: hard lids (`OBSERVED_ON∘DATED_AS`) date the paper; fingerprint keeps edition text with production HTR (right text, often wrong edition date). Treating “re-align under hard lids” as a dating precondition forces those channels to reconcile before either may advance.
- **User:** agreed favour options **(2) inherit date via composition** + **(3) decouple pin promote from content re-align**; soften **(1)** (content may propose dating) only where hard lids are absent.
- **Decision:**
  1. **Inherit date (composition, not content-as-dating).** Once `ALIGNED_TO` identifies the HTR session that holds the text, the sitting date for that placement is **inherited** from that session’s hard dating path (`Session -OBSERVED_ON-> Scan -DATED_AS-> SittingDay`, equivalently the active `PINNED_TO:hard` bead). Content selects the session; dating rides the archival claim set. Fingerprint / neighbourhood / overflow **never** write `PINNED_TO`.
  2. **Decouple pin promote.** Overwriting the production `PINNED_TO` key (and concordance `resolved_session_id`) gates on hard evidence + monotone within the inventory (+ human folio where contested) — **not** on content re-align finishing. Content re-align under hard lids remains legitimate work as a *consumer* of those lids (`ALIGNED_TO`, separation metrics); it is no longer a precondition that unlocks dating.
  3. **Soften only in gaps.** Where hard lids are absent, content may *propose* a pin candidate that still requires an independent dating channel (heading / physical / human folio) before any write — necessary ≠ sufficient. Where hard lids exist, content does not propose dating at all.
- **Reason:** Keeps the spine’s edge separation (dating vs identity) while removing the procedural knot that made production pins permanently stale. Matches the measured lag reading: edition date is often wrong relative to the paper; the fix is to attach text to the session that holds it and take that session’s hard date, not to let similarity scores invent calendar ownership. Pin promote can proceed on evidence that already passed monotone on the seed window (`hard_pin_repin`, 17 edges).
- **Supersedes:** PLAN / STATE / SEQUENCE_MODEL wording that made “content re-align under hard lids” the NEXT gate before production pin overwrite. Content re-align is not cancelled; it no longer unlocks dating.
- **Out of scope (unchanged):** uncertain `num-150` cut gold (count mismatch); bulk `--on-neighbourhood` until seed pin promote settles; preceding-scan channel.
- **Next implementable:** ~~scoped production `PINNED_TO` promote~~ **DONE**; ~~place under lids~~ **DONE** (stages 8–9 folded into procedure). Seed: separated 142→181 (Δ+39), corpus +39, vs uniform −3; 184 `ALIGNED_TO:hard_place` wired. Pin alone was Δ0; placement under lids is the measured buy.

## 2026-10-12: Remediation stages 8–9 are part of the procedure (not optional)

- **Context:** Stage 7 pin promote landed dating on the graph with Δ separated = 0. Placement under lids looked bolted on; user required integration that measurably improves the backbone.
- **Decision:** Extend the remediation track to stages 7–9 (pin → place under lids → wire concordance + `ALIGNED_TO:hard_place`). Gate stage 8/9 on slice `separated`↑ and corpus non-decrease; always report vs uniform. Every later lag window runs through stage 9.
- **Measured (seed 1626-09-21..10-25):** PASS — 142→181 separated (Δ+39), corpus 8487→8526; after vs uniform −3. Pack exclusive starts on pinned session streams when not already fully separated.
- **Measured (Nov 1626-11-03..11-12):** stage 7 PASS (6 pins wired, Δ0). Stage 8 first FAIL (48→47) was **heading-only room**: `DATED_AS` only on date-heading pages; folio shows 4 Nov continuing on 450–451 (Jovis 5 Nov opens f446v / scan 451) but those scans have no `OBSERVED_ON`/HTR in graph. **Fix:** place uses sitting scan-span (`DATED_AS` .. before next `DATED_AS`) and **abstains when `room < k_e`**. Re-run PASS 48→59 (Δ+11), corpus +11; `11-04` abstained; 5 other dates wired.
- **Next:** upstream HTR/session coverage for continuation scans in span; do not invent `DATED_AS` on undated continuation pages.

## 2026-10-13: Sliding-window boundary inference beats 512 truncation on overflow gold

- **Context:** Overflow encoder cuts looked oddly spaced; long flats (num-150: 753 tok) were truncated at 512 so all top-k fired in the visible prefix; 2/3 human gold cuts on num-150 sat past the window.
- **Tried:** `trunc_512` (legacy) · `win_512_s256` · `win_512_s128` · `win_512_s256_gap` · `even_split` on `overflow_cut_gold` (n=2) + short controls (n=2).
- **Result:** **`win_512_s256` best** — overflow F1@50 **0.833** (trunc 0.500); num-150 pred `[1257, 3262, 3394]` ≈ gold `[1257, 3263, 3397]`. Short controls F1=1.0 (no regression). Gap NMS and denser stride did not help.
- **Decision:** Use stride-256 overflowing windows for encoder cut proposals on long HTR; keep trunc path only as ablation. Dataset `boundary_windowed_eval`.
- **Code:** `scripts/boundary_windowed_infer.py`, `scripts/eval_boundary_windowed.py`; wired into overflow encoder propose path.

## 2026-10-13: Phase-1 acceptance closed; theoretical optimum is splitting-conditional

- **Context:** All four Phase-1 Tier O criteria (2026-09-22) are MET: separated **9,550** = **82.0%** of the paragraph ceiling **11,644**; Global spans gap=2 **9 / 445**; Local 3185–3189 clear. Stretch ≥75% and primary ≥50% are exhausted as steering instruments. Remaining “optional early-1626 / `11-04` HTR” is mop-up under the **old** ceiling, not a new theory of success. Track P P0 already warned that re-paragraphing after resolution splits **raises the denominator** while improving real coverage.
- **Decision:**
  1. **Phase-1 criteria closed MET** — keep the 2026-09-22 table as historical; do not raise primary/stretch % in place against 11,644.
  2. **Theoretical optimum = f(resolution splitting).** Report three nested horizons, never one collapsed %: (a) hard structural / no usable main-series HTR (~5,590 `k_e`, splitting-invariant); (b) Phase-1 optimum = 100% of **11,644** on today’s paragraph axis (residual ~**2,094**); (c) Phase-2 optimum after re-paragraph — ceiling rises by at least the pigeonhole-blocked ~**1,886** on 390 crowded days, and up to the larger collision mass ~**4,516** shared starts (do not conflate). Secret series stays out of these denominators.
  3. **Dual ceiling reporting** is required in Tier O / `project_overview`. Track P P0 ceiling-rise sign-off was still load-bearing here; **signed off same day** (entry below). `extent ≤ 3` may need recalibration after re-paragraph (~273 currently-separated at risk per Track P).
  4. **Adjudication unchanged:** `separated` still rules vs gold when they disagree; every `separated` claim still vs `uniform_start_paragraphs`. aDNA residual rule stays — classify what sits below Phase-2, do not omit it.
  5. **Live steering** = Phase-2 horizons + residual typology; early-1626 / `11-04` = optional Phase-1 mop-up only.
- **Reason:** Acceptance cleared the bar that justified the hard-pin remediation track. Further `separated` gains under a fixed paragraph stream are diminishing mop-up; the next load-bearing question is whether splitting re-paragraphs the axis. Stating optimum as splitting-conditional prevents mistaking Phase-1 residual for “need a cut inside a paragraph,” and prevents reporting Phase-2 coverage against a stale 11,644 denominator.

## 2026-10-13: Track P P0: Phase-2 ceiling may rise under re-paragraph

- **Context:** Track P P0 was the remaining gate before P3: re-paragraphing after mid-paragraph cuts raises the pigeonhole denominator above 11,644 while real coverage rises. Phase-1 stretch % against 11,644 are retired (DECISIONS 2026-10-13). Measured cost: +2,919 paragraphs (~13.6% of axis) on 1,597 colliding paragraphs; unlocks up to ~4,516 collision-blocked starts (pigeonhole-blocked floor ~1,886 on 390 crowded days); ~809 currently-separated overlap a to-be-split paragraph and ~273 (3.4% of separated) may then exceed extent<=3. separated stays paragraph-keyed; no rekey to (paragraph, char_offset).
- **Decision:** (1) Sign off that the Phase-2 ceiling may rise: report separated and stretch against the post-re-paragraph paragraph stream (dual ceiling with the historical 11,644), never treat a falling headline % on a larger denominator as regression. (2) License P3: carry char offsets through build_anchor_backbone/segment_gap so one paragraph can host several starts; score separated vs uniform_start_paragraphs (exact agreement only); gold/synthetic as diagnostic. (3) Version the axis successor with provenance — no in-place mutation of paragraph_start_index streams. (4) Revisit MAX_EXTENT_PARAGRAPHS=3 meaning on the ~7.4% re-paragraphed mass as part of P3 migration, not a blocker to starting.
- **Reason:** The trade is measured (~16:1 unlock vs extent risk) and already framed as Phase-2 splitting-conditional optimum. Choosing it explicitly unlocks the only remaining Phase-2 ceiling lever; delaying P0 while P1 PASS and P2 synthetic gold exist only stalls the track on a known denominator effect.

## 2026-10-13: Track P P4: predicted Group-C offsets beat plain collisions

- **Decision:** Accept s6c_p4_predicted_offset_eval as P4 diagnostic PASS: 590 days, separated 6324 vs plain 4592 vs uniform-on-successor 6194 (delta +1732 vs plain; 501/590 beat plain; 190/590 beat uniform). Cut fill 0.435; extent>3=231. Do not yet persist production axis — next is versioned successor write + cut-fill/extent gate. Dual ceiling (12048/14113) unchanged.

## 2026-10-13: Same-day content-run flag + tier viewer (not monotone)

- **Context:** Hand review found `1629-05-12` pin `session-3188-num-124` (axis dated 05-13) while trusted/exact and calendar HTR are `num-123`. Monotone PASS (122→124→125); content wholesale mismatch. Review cannot look beyond the current day.
- **Decision:** Add a **same-day content verdict** with consecutive-run rule (`CONTENT_RUN_K=3`) on pin stream vs calendar stream — complement to hard_pin monotone, **not** a replacement and **not** yet a promote refuse gate. Twin flag: concordance pin ∉ trusted/exact. Ship in `p5-axis-viewer`: meta pills, `/tier/<date>` (editorial · pin HTR · calendar HTR, edges with hover text), and `uv run python -m p5_axis_viewer.report --from … --to … [--write]` → `p5_day_sync_report`.
- **Reason:** Off-by-one pins that stay monotone are invisible to `hard_pins_monotone`; consecutive content fail (and calendar_ok_pin_fail) is the human-visible same-day signal. Reporting makes the flag corpus-window measurable before any gate change.
- **Edge:** content_run on pin axis crosses PINNED_TO (inferred); calendar stream is same-day OBSERVED structure only.

## 2026-10-13: Track P P5: gated versioned axis successor (persist path)

- **Context:** P4 unlocked collisions but under-proposed (fill 0.435) and hit extent>3=231. Production needed a versioned successor with gates, not a blind write of all predicted cuts.
- **Decision:** Adopt `scripts/s6c_p5_persist_axis_successor.py`. Defaults: host gate `enough_cuts` (drop partial proposals); day gate `beats_plain_and_no_extent_regression`. Outputs `paragraph_axis_p5_predicted_v1` + `s6c_p5_axis_successor_ledger`; parent `paragraph_axis_1626_1630` never mutated; main series + scope bar at entry.
- **Full write (2026-10-13):** 590 colliding days → **376 accepted / 214 rejected**; **661** cuts; axis **18946→19607**; separated accepted days **4454 vs plain 3099 (Δ+1355)**; extent>3 **155≤158**; gated fill **0.339**. Next: dual-ceiling Tier O on the successor.
- **Reason:** Gates address the two production risks P4 measured (weak cut fill, extent regression) while preserving P0's versioned-successor rule.
- **Edge:** successor paragraphs are HTR CONTAINS structure; placement claims that cross PINNED_TO remain inferred.
- **Metrics:** `track-p-subparagraph-division/p5_days_accepted` = 376; `track-p-subparagraph-division/p5_delta_vs_plain_accepted` = 1355; `track-p-subparagraph-division/p5_cuts_applied` = 661; `track-p-subparagraph-division/p5_n_paragraphs_after` = 19607 (as of 2026-10-13)
